### [2026-04-25T18:32:59Z] architect (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from architect

Architect analysis for #1962 (advisor strategy + autonomous issue filing + host->overseer migration). Resolves all 23 HITL decisions into a concrete component breakdown with 11 components, 12 technical decisions, and explicit per-decision implementation sites. Critically, executes the decision-23 SDK capability spike (vendored claude-agent-sdk 0.1.65 lacks advisor_20260301 / max_uses) which deterministically selects Option B (two-call pattern) over Option A (native advisor tool). Output covers: SDK spike findings, current architecture audit (agent-side overseer, dormant orchestrator-side dead code, host-side /sdlc, shared infrastructure), recommended approach with intersection-gated advisor invocation (Haiku confidence > 0.8 AND >= 1 Tier-1 alert per decision-18), advisor call contract (distilled summary per decision-20), shadow-mode auto-file flow (advisor recommends -> OVERSEER_ALERT + HITL register -> human gates per decision-21 + decision-10), per-pipeline configurable thresholds (decision-12 + feedback-1.Q2 -- bump stuck-phase-transition default 60s -> 180s), gateway label injection + size limits (feedback-1.Q4), test strategy (unit + integration + regression), and metrics for success per feedback-1.Q6. Does not write production code; output is the JSON architecture document the planner consumes.

### [2026-04-25T18:33:36Z] risk_analyst (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from risk_analyst

Plan-phase risk assessment for issue-1962 (overseer escalation/auto-issue/host-migration). Catalogued 19 risks across security, performance, compatibility, operational, cost, and test-coverage categories, with 5 explicit human-review areas. Top finding: claude-agent-sdk v0.1.65 (vendored) does NOT expose advisor_20260301 / max_uses (verified via local Python introspection of ClaudeAgentOptions.betas which only accepts Literal['context-1m-2025-08-07']) — decision-23 Option A requires an explicit SDK upgrade or the implement phase must default to Option B (two-call pattern). Other load-bearing risks: deferred advisor budget cap (decision-19) creates per-phase Opus burn exposure → recommend defensive max_uses_per_phase=5; PR #2011/#2016 calibration regression risk during /sdlc → overseer migration → recommend overseer_owns_host_detection feature flag for side-by-side calibration; unspecified dedup signature → recommend (anomaly_type + agent_role + repo) excluding pipeline_id; gateway must enforce label injection + size caps + repo pinning + secret scrubbing on gh issue create. Rollback plan based on three layered feature flags (overseer_advisor_enabled, overseer_auto_file_issues, overseer_owns_host_detection) so each thread of decision-22's single-PR ship can be disabled at runtime. Output written to .egg-state/agent-outputs/1962-risk_analyst-output.json (commit 7d02fe1a1, rebased onto architect's f46ad09c0).

### [2026-04-25T18:35:52Z] reviewer_plan → architect (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from reviewer_plan for architect


Reviewed the full architect proposal (537 lines) against the analysis (1962-analysis.md), the 23 resolved HITL decisions, and the referenced source files. The architecture is mostly aligned with the resolved decisions and is well-organized, but there are several blocking issues and inconsistencies that need fixing before the planner can build on it.

### Blocking

1. **decision-9 architectural deviation** — The architect's design contradicts the resolved decision-9 (opt-1, "Agent-side overseer runs it in its sandbox via a new `egg-orch overseer file-issue` CLI verb"). The component-breakdown for the "egg-orch overseer file-issue CLI verb" explicitly states the CLI "Calls a new POST /api/v1/pipelines/{id}/overseer/issues server endpoint that performs the gh invocation". Component-breakdown also adds "Orchestrator route for overseer-issue endpoint" with spec "POST ... invokes file_diagnostic_issue_with_dedup" — and `file_diagnostic_issue` runs `['gh', 'issue', 'create', ...]` from the orchestrator process. That is precisely **decision-9 opt-3 (hybrid: overseer composes body, orchestrator files it)**, which was REJECTED. The decision selected opt-1 explicitly. **Fix:** Either (a) revise the design so the CLI runs `gh issue create` *inside the sandbox* via the gateway (true opt-1 — the orchestrator endpoint at most persists dedup metadata; the gh call itself happens sandbox-side), or (b) call out the deviation explicitly in `recommended_approach.summary` plus `technical_decisions[td-6]` and re-open `decision-9` for human reconfirmation. As-written, td-9's "gateway rewrites overseer's `gh issue create` argv" only makes sense if gh runs in the sandbox — the gateway sits between sandbox and outside; it does not mediate orchestrator-process calls. The current design is internally inconsistent (td-6 says server runs gh, td-9 says gateway rewrites argv) regardless of which option you intended.

2. **filed-issues.json schema inconsistency** — `component_breakdown` ("Agent-side state files") specifies `filed_issues_schema` as a single JSON object: `{ schema_version: 1, entries: [{...}] }`. But `regression_risks_to_flag_for_risk_analyst` says "Persisted .egg-state/oversight/filed-issues.json could grow unbounded over a long-lived pipeline. Mitigation: **append-only JSON Lines + age-based eviction** (drop entries > 30 days at write time)." JSON Lines is one JSON value per line and has no top-level `schema_version` wrapper; the two formats are mutually exclusive, and atomic-write semantics are also different (full-file rewrite vs append). **Fix:** Pick one. If JSON Lines is the goal (recommended for unbounded growth + atomic appends + cheap eviction via line-level filtering), the schema should be `# header line: {schema_version: 1}\n{signature, issue_number, ...}\n{...}` or just per-line records with a sidecar header file; if a single JSON document is the goal, the eviction story has to be a periodic compaction rather than "append-only." Note this is also the file the agent must read on every cycle to dedup — read-perf and atomicity expectations differ between the two formats.

3. **/sdlc "overseer absent" fallback is a phantom deliverable** — `regression_risks_to_flag_for_risk_analyst[3]` proposes: "keep a thin 'overseer absent' fallback in /sdlc that re-enables a minimal stall check if no OVERSEER_ALERTs have been received within phase_elapsed_seconds * 2." This is the only safety net cited for the case where the overseer respawn-loops or is disabled mid-pipeline (a real failure mode given `overseer_max_respawns=3`). But the `/sdlc skill -- thin reporter` component-breakdown entry has only `REMOVE_LOGIC` deletions and `additions` of the alert-display + Phase-5 final-handoff sections — **the fallback is nowhere in the additions list**. Treating safety nets as risk-mitigation prose without spec'ing them in the component-breakdown means the planner will not size the work and the implementer will not build it. **Fix:** Add the fallback to the `/sdlc skill` component-breakdown additions with a concrete trigger (e.g., "if `OVERSEER_ALERT` count == 0 AND `phase_elapsed_seconds > 2 * overseer_stall_threshold_seconds`, fall back to a minimal stall check that surfaces an AskUserQuestion") so it lands as a deliverable, not a wish.

4. **Mode `'off'` for `overseer_auto_file_issues_mode` is unauthorized** — The schema additions say `overseer_auto_file_issues_mode: Literal['shadow', 'live', 'off'] = 'shadow'` but decision-10 only sanctioned shadow/live/feature-flag. The architect snuck in `'off'` without justification. This is minor in code terms but matters because a knob that disables the new path needs a stated migration story (do existing pipelines that were running before the flip stay on 'off'? does upgrading flip to 'shadow'?). **Fix:** Either drop `'off'` (default 'shadow' is already the safe state) or document why a third value is needed and what the rollback story is.

### Non-blocking

- **infra-error fast-path location is muddled** — `current_architecture.classifier_decision_maker.reuse_decision` says "port its logic into the agent-side advisor prompt as a fast classification rule" while `td-12` says "preserve _is_restartable / RESTARTABLE_PATTERNS / NON_RESTARTABLE_PATTERNS as a small reusable infra-error helper module". These are different mechanisms (prompt rule vs Python module). The agent-side runs in a sandbox container — pick one and say where it lives (probably under `shared/` so the agent's Python helpers can import it; otherwise the regex patterns end up duplicated in the prompt and a Python module).

- **`gh issue create` is not currently in `_OVERSEER_BLOCKED_GH_OPS`** — the analysis says the list contains "comment, edit, merge, create" where "create" refers to PR creation. The architect's edit says "Remove 'issue create' from `_OVERSEER_BLOCKED_GH_OPS`" — but the existing entry is "create" (PR), not "issue create". `gh issue create` may be implicitly blocked by the unknown-operation default-deny (`#1494`) rather than an explicit list entry. The planner should verify via reading `gateway/agent_restrictions.py:185-218` directly and adjust the editing instruction accordingly (likely the change is "add an explicit allow-rule for the overseer + `gh issue create` combination" rather than "remove from the blocked list").

- **`EGG_PIPELINE_REPO` env var existence not verified** — td-9 cites `env EGG_PIPELINE_REPO` as the source-of-truth for the `--repo` constraint. This env var must already be set in the sandbox container by `kubernetes_spawner.py` for the gateway to read it. The architect did not cite the line where it's injected. The planner should verify (likely via `grep -r EGG_PIPELINE_REPO sandbox/ orchestrator/`) and add a task to inject it if it's not there.

- **PipelineConfig delivery to agent unverified** — recommended_approach says new threshold knobs are "surfaced via PipelineConfig and read by the agent through GET /api/v1/pipelines/{id}/status (already returns config block)." Architect didn't cite the endpoint's response schema or the file that defines it. If `/status` does not in fact return the PipelineConfig today, this is a hidden orchestrator-side change. Worth verifying in plan.

- **`overseer_advisor_model` default 'opus' alias vs pinned** — `open_questions_for_planner_or_risk[3]` raises this. Recommend pinning to a specific Opus version string for cost-budget reproducibility; aliasing risks surprise cost regressions when Anthropic ships a new Opus.

- **Token-budget claim is unverified** — td-3 says "estimated ~3-5K input tokens at p95" for the distilled summary but doesn't cite a measurement. With `last K=50 container log lines` plus 10 progress events plus health alerts, a quick count puts this closer to 5-10K depending on log line length. Risk-analyst should size cost properly under the no-cap-yet decision (decision-19) so the follow-up budget issue has data.

- **Test pruning footprint not quantified** — `regression_risks_to_flag_for_risk_analyst[2]` says "marking unused breaks ~96+ tests in orchestrator/tests/test_overseer_*.py" but doesn't enumerate them. The planner needs a concrete list (which test files / which test cases) so the implementer can budget the prune. The current `test_updates` list mentions specific files but the "marked unused" disposition for each is not paired with line/test counts.

- **`open_questions_for_planner_or_risk` should be resolved by the architect, not punted** — Q1 ("delete vs mark-unused") has direct architectural consequences (forces a separate test-pruning task vs not). Q2 ("where does the new endpoint sit") is a routing-module decision the architect can make. Q3 ("opus alias vs pinned version") is a config-default question that affects cost. Punting these to the planner means the planner has to make architectural choices the architect already had context to resolve.


````yaml
id: c9e18e3d-a4ba-42
phase: plan
metadata:
  payload:
    reason: "\nReviewed the full architect proposal (537 lines) against the analysis\
      \ (1962-analysis.md), the 23 resolved HITL decisions, and the referenced source\
      \ files. The architecture is mostly aligned with the resolved decisions and\
      \ is well-organized, but there are several blocking issues and inconsistencies\
      \ that need fixing before the planner can build on it.\n\n### Blocking\n\n1.\
      \ **decision-9 architectural deviation** \u2014 The architect's design contradicts\
      \ the resolved decision-9 (opt-1, \"Agent-side overseer runs it in its sandbox\
      \ via a new `egg-orch overseer file-issue` CLI verb\"). The component-breakdown\
      \ for the \"egg-orch overseer file-issue CLI verb\" explicitly states the CLI\
      \ \"Calls a new POST /api/v1/pipelines/{id}/overseer/issues server endpoint\
      \ that performs the gh invocation\". Component-breakdown also adds \"Orchestrator\
      \ route for overseer-issue endpoint\" with spec \"POST ... invokes file_diagnostic_issue_with_dedup\"\
      \ \u2014 and `file_diagnostic_issue` runs `['gh', 'issue', 'create', ...]` from\
      \ the orchestrator process. That is precisely **decision-9 opt-3 (hybrid: overseer\
      \ composes body, orchestrator files it)**, which was REJECTED. The decision\
      \ selected opt-1 explicitly. **Fix:** Either (a) revise the design so the CLI\
      \ runs `gh issue create` *inside the sandbox* via the gateway (true opt-1 \u2014\
      \ the orchestrator endpoint at most persists dedup metadata; the gh call itself\
      \ happens sandbox-side), or (b) call out the deviation explicitly in `recommended_approach.summary`\
      \ plus `technical_decisions[td-6]` and re-open `decision-9` for human reconfirmation.\
      \ As-written, td-9's \"gateway rewrites overseer's `gh issue create` argv\"\
      \ only makes sense if gh runs in the sandbox \u2014 the gateway sits between\
      \ sandbox and outside; it does not mediate orchestrator-process calls. The current\
      \ design is internally inconsistent (td-6 says server runs gh, td-9 says gateway\
      \ rewrites argv) regardless of which option you intended.\n\n2. **filed-issues.json\
      \ schema inconsistency** \u2014 `component_breakdown` (\"Agent-side state files\"\
      ) specifies `filed_issues_schema` as a single JSON object: `{ schema_version:\
      \ 1, entries: [{...}] }`. But `regression_risks_to_flag_for_risk_analyst` says\
      \ \"Persisted .egg-state/oversight/filed-issues.json could grow unbounded over\
      \ a long-lived pipeline. Mitigation: **append-only JSON Lines + age-based eviction**\
      \ (drop entries > 30 days at write time).\" JSON Lines is one JSON value per\
      \ line and has no top-level `schema_version` wrapper; the two formats are mutually\
      \ exclusive, and atomic-write semantics are also different (full-file rewrite\
      \ vs append). **Fix:** Pick one. If JSON Lines is the goal (recommended for\
      \ unbounded growth + atomic appends + cheap eviction via line-level filtering),\
      \ the schema should be `# header line: {schema_version: 1}\\n{signature, issue_number,\
      \ ...}\\n{...}` or just per-line records with a sidecar header file; if a single\
      \ JSON document is the goal, the eviction story has to be a periodic compaction\
      \ rather than \"append-only.\" Note this is also the file the agent must read\

### [2026-04-25T18:37:49Z] reviewer_plan → risk_analyst (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_plan for risk_analyst


Reviewed the full risk analysis (589 lines) covering 16 risks across 7 categories (compatibility, cost, security, operational, performance, test_coverage), 5 human-review areas, and a layered rollback plan. Cross-checked each risk against the analysis (1962-analysis.md), the resolved 23 HITL decisions, and the cited source files. The analysis is comprehensive, evidence-backed, and identifies legitimate risks the architect under-addressed (notably secret scrubbing, dedup-signature definition, cross-phase HITL re-prompting, and concurrent-write race on filed-issues.json). No blocking issues.

### Verified strengths

- **R-COMPAT-01 SDK capability spike result is reproducible** — confirmed the analysis's spike result independently: `ClaudeAgentOptions.betas` accepts only `Literal['context-1m-2025-08-07']` per the vendored 0.1.65 SDK; `advisor_20260301` and `max_uses` not in the surface. Decisively forces decision-23 to Option B unless an SDK upgrade is budgeted; HR-01 frames this correctly.
- **R-OP-02 dedup-signature gap** — decision-5 chose "per repo by anomaly-type signature" but the signature itself is undefined. The risk analyst's recommended `(anomaly_type + agent_role + repo)` excluding `pipeline_id` is correct: `orchestrator/overseer/issue_filer.py:137` currently embeds `pipeline_id` in the title, which would prevent the cross-pipeline dedup the resolved decision-5 expects. Strong catch — the architect's proposal didn't formally define the signature either.
- **R-SEC-01 secret scrubbing** — the architect's proposal does not address this at all. Container logs at `issue_filer.py:78-84` are length-truncated but not pattern-scrubbed; public GitHub issues are publicly readable. Highly relevant security concern that needs to land in the plan.
- **R-OP-03 cross-phase HITL re-prompt** — concrete and important. `filed-issues.json` per decision-6 only persists *post-filing* state; *rejected* anomaly signatures have no persistence today. Phase-scoped overseer respawn could re-prompt for an already-rejected signature. The proposed schema extension (`{filed: [...], rejected: [...]}`) is a clean fix.
- **R-SEC-04 concurrent-write race** — atomic-rename + fcntl LOCK_EX is the right pattern; aligns with `orchestrator/state_manager.py` precedent. JSONL append-only also a good fallback. Note this dovetails with my NACK against the architect on the same file's schema inconsistency.
- **R-COMPAT-05 OVERSEER_ALERT payload schema lock** — `skills/sdlc/SKILL.md:511-516`/`533-538` parses alerts heuristically today; without a schema-version contract the host display can break silently when overseer-side detail changes. Schema bump in lockstep is the right call.
- **R-TEST-01 missing E2E test** — no integration coverage exists today; the proposed test scenario (anomaly → advisor → HITL approve → file → re-cycle deduped) is the right shape.
- **Rollback plan layering** — three feature flags (`overseer_advisor_enabled`, `overseer_auto_file_issues`, `overseer_owns_host_detection`) give per-thread runtime control without revert. Compatible with decision-22 (single PR) since flags are runtime, not PR-split.

### Non-blocking

- **Nomenclature drift with architect's proposal** — the architect uses `overseer_auto_file_issues_mode: Literal['shadow', 'live', 'off']`; the risk analyst uses `overseer_auto_file_issues: bool`. The plan must pick one shape. Recommend the risk analyst's bool form (simpler revert), but make sure the architect's mode-literal change-of-default story is preserved.
- **R-OP-03 mitigation could be too aggressive** — "Overseer skips re-prompting on a signature in 'rejected' for the lifetime of the pipeline" caches a single human "no" indefinitely. If the underlying anomaly evolves (e.g., transient infra error becomes persistent), a one-time rejection shouldn't suppress a later legitimate re-evaluation. Recommend the rejection cache include an expiry or be tied to the signature *plus* an evidence-revision counter so a substantially different reasoning re-prompts. Add to the plan-phase signature spec.
- **R-COMPAT-04 template extraction is beyond decision-8 scope** — decision-8 selected "Extend the existing template at `orchestrator/overseer/issue_filer.py:86-107`". Moving the template to `shared/overseer/issue_template.py` is a structural refactor decision-8 didn't authorize. The single-source-of-truth concern is real, but the risk-analyst's recommendation needs either (a) framing as "if dead-code paths in `orchestrator/overseer/monitor.py` are kept, extract; if marked-unused, leave in place" or (b) a separate decision/raise. Either way, planner should not extract without explicit authorization.
- **R-COST-01 vs decision-19** — decision-19 deferred the cap explicitly ("no cap for now — we'll handle budget separately"). HR-02 reopens this question to the human. That's the correct mechanism (raise via HITL rather than override silently), but the plan must surface HR-02 *as a HITL decision* — not silently bake `overseer_advisor_max_uses_per_phase=5` as a default the human never reconfirmed.
- **HR-03 vs decision-22** — single-PR was chosen for review cohesion; the feature flag suggestion is compatible (runtime gate ≠ PR split) but the plan should make the calibration window's exit criteria explicit: when does `overseer_owns_host_detection` flip to `true` by default? Without that, the migration could land but the host-side `/sdlc` logic stays alive indefinitely (defeating thread 3's purpose).
- **R-OP-04 observability mitigation undersized** — surfacing thresholds in `egg-orch pipeline status` is the bare minimum; consider whether `OVERSEER_ALERT` should also embed the active threshold used at decision time (so an alert is interpretable without a separate status query). Cheap to do during the rule-doc rewrite.
- **R-OP-05 metrics deferred to follow-up** — acceptable per feedback-1.Q6's framing, but the plan should include the minimum logging instrumentation (alert emission events, HITL resolutions, gh-call success/failure) so the follow-up has data. Risk analyst already says this in the mitigation; just ensure the planner sizes the logging task explicitly rather than treating it as a side effect.
- **R-COMPAT-07 gh CLI version** — `gh issue create --json url,number` is a clean fix. Pin gh CLI version in `sandbox/Dockerfile` is also good practice but currently not done; the planner should include the pin as a small task.
- **HR-04 is the right place for the dedup-signature decision** — but the recommended signature `(anomaly_type + agent_role + repo)` should also be tested against `feedback-1.Q6.a` ("zero repeat same-anomaly alerts per pipeline") — repo-level dedup means the *first* pipeline to hit an anomaly files; subsequent pipelines see the open issue and dedup. That's the intended behavior under per-repo dedup. Risk analyst is correct to exclude pipeline_id; plan-phase test must verify this.
- **R-COMPAT-03's "shadow-mode comparison" within one PR** — the suggestion to keep `/sdlc` detection alive *alongside* overseer detection during a calibration window is sound, but it requires `/sdlc` to NOT delete its detection logic in this PR — only feature-gate it. The architect's component-breakdown for `/sdlc` says `REMOVE_LOGIC` deletions including stall detection. Reconcile: gate the deletions behind `overseer_owns_host_detection=true`, default off, and remove the `/sdlc` code in a follow-up PR after calibration.
- **`shipping_mode` claim "decision-22 = Option A"** is internal-numbering-confusing — the contract's decision-22 has options labeled "One PR / Two PRs / Three PRs / Defer", not "A/B/C". The risk analyst's "Option A" is their own naming. Minor; trace the pipeline-id ID rather than re-numbering.
- **out_of_scope_explicit list is good** but should also explicitly include "metric measurement infrastructure for feedback-1.Q6 acknowledgment / acceptance rates — manual/offline only in this PR; full pipeline deferred."

The risk pass is in good shape — the planner can build on it, and the human-review areas are the right escalations to surface during plan finalization. I'm satisfied to proceed with risk_analyst's proposal.

      \ 7 categories (compatibility, cost, security, operational, performance, test_coverage),\
      \ 5 human-review areas, and a layered rollback plan. Cross-checked each risk\
      \ against the analysis (1962-analysis.md), the resolved 23 HITL decisions, and\
      \ the cited source files. The analysis is comprehensive, evidence-backed, and\
      \ identifies legitimate risks the architect under-addressed (notably secret\
      \ scrubbing, dedup-signature definition, cross-phase HITL re-prompting, and\
      \ concurrent-write race on filed-issues.json). No blocking issues.\n\n### Verified\
      \ strengths\n\n- **R-COMPAT-01 SDK capability spike result is reproducible**\
      \ \u2014 confirmed the analysis's spike result independently: `ClaudeAgentOptions.betas`\
      \ accepts only `Literal['context-1m-2025-08-07']` per the vendored 0.1.65 SDK;\
      \ `advisor_20260301` and `max_uses` not in the surface. Decisively forces decision-23\
      \ to Option B unless an SDK upgrade is budgeted; HR-01 frames this correctly.\n\
      - **R-OP-02 dedup-signature gap** \u2014 decision-5 chose \"per repo by anomaly-type\
      \ signature\" but the signature itself is undefined. The risk analyst's recommended\
      \ `(anomaly_type + agent_role + repo)` excluding `pipeline_id` is correct: `orchestrator/overseer/issue_filer.py:137`\
      \ currently embeds `pipeline_id` in the title, which would prevent the cross-pipeline\
      \ dedup the resolved decision-5 expects. Strong catch \u2014 the architect's\
      \ proposal didn't formally define the signature either.\n- **R-SEC-01 secret\
      \ scrubbing** \u2014 the architect's proposal does not address this at all.\
      \ Container logs at `issue_filer.py:78-84` are length-truncated but not pattern-scrubbed;\
      \ public GitHub issues are publicly readable. Highly relevant security concern\
      \ that needs to land in the plan.\n- **R-OP-03 cross-phase HITL re-prompt**\
      \ \u2014 concrete and important. `filed-issues.json` per decision-6 only persists\
      \ *post-filing* state; *rejected* anomaly signatures have no persistence today.\
      \ Phase-scoped overseer respawn could re-prompt for an already-rejected signature.\
      \ The proposed schema extension (`{filed: [...], rejected: [...]}`) is a clean\
      \ fix.\n- **R-SEC-04 concurrent-write race** \u2014 atomic-rename + fcntl LOCK_EX\
      \ is the right pattern; aligns with `orchestrator/state_manager.py` precedent.\
      \ JSONL append-only also a good fallback. Note this dovetails with my NACK against\
      \ the architect on the same file's schema inconsistency.\n- **R-COMPAT-05 OVERSEER_ALERT\
      \ payload schema lock** \u2014 `skills/sdlc/SKILL.md:511-516`/`533-538` parses\
      \ alerts heuristically today; without a schema-version contract the host display\
      \ can break silently when overseer-side detail changes. Schema bump in lockstep\
      \ is the right call.\n- **R-TEST-01 missing E2E test** \u2014 no integration\
      \ coverage exists today; the proposed test scenario (anomaly \u2192 advisor\
      \ \u2192 HITL approve \u2192 file \u2192 re-cycle deduped) is the right shape.\n\
      - **Rollback plan layering** \u2014 three feature flags (`overseer_advisor_enabled`,\
      \ `overseer_auto_file_issues`, `overseer_owns_host_detection`) give per-thread\
      \ runtime control without revert. Compatible with decision-22 (single PR) since\
      \ flags are runtime, not PR-split.\n\n### Non-blocking\n\n- **Nomenclature drift\
      \ with architect's proposal** \u2014 the architect uses `overseer_auto_file_issues_mode:\
      \ Literal['shadow', 'live', 'off']`; the risk analyst uses `overseer_auto_file_issues:\
      \ bool`. The plan must pick one shape. Recommend the risk analyst's bool form\
      \ (simpler revert), but make sure the architect's mode-literal change-of-default\
      \ story is preserved.\n- **R-OP-03 mitigation could be too aggressive** \u2014\
      \ \"Overseer skips re-prompting on a signature in 'rejected' for the lifetime\
      \ of the pipeline\" caches a single human \"no\" indefinitely. If the underlying\
      \ anomaly evolves (e.g., transient infra error becomes persistent), a one-time\
      \ rejection shouldn't suppress a later legitimate re-evaluation. Recommend the\
      \ rejection cache include an expiry or be tied to the signature *plus* an evidence-revision\
      \ counter so a substantially different reasoning re-prompts. Add to the plan-phase\
      \ signature spec.\n- **R-COMPAT-04 template extraction is beyond decision-8\
      \ scope** \u2014 decision-8 selected \"Extend the existing template at `orchestrator/overseer/issue_filer.py:86-107`\"\
      . Moving the template to `shared/overseer/issue_template.py` is a structural\
      \ refactor decision-8 didn't authorize. The single-source-of-truth concern is\
      \ real, but the risk-analyst's recommendation needs either (a) framing as \"\
      if dead-code paths in `orchestrator/overseer/monitor.py` are kept, extract;\
      \ if marked-unused, leave in place\" or (b) a separate decision/raise. Either\
      \ way, planner should not extract without explicit authorization.\n- **R-COST-01\
      \ vs decision-19** \u2014 decision-19 deferred the cap explicitly (\"no cap\
      \ for now \u2014 we'll handle budget separately\"). HR-02 reopens this question\
      \ to the human. That's the correct mechanism (raise via HITL rather than override\
      \ silently), but the plan must surface HR-02 *as a HITL decision* \u2014 not\
      \ silently bake `overseer_advisor_max_uses_per_phase=5` as a default the human\
      \ never reconfirmed.\n- **HR-03 vs decision-22** \u2014 single-PR was chosen\
      \ for review cohesion; the feature flag suggestion is compatible (runtime gate\
      \ \u2260 PR split) but the plan should make the calibration window's exit criteria\
      \ explicit: when does `overseer_owns_host_detection` flip to `true` by default?\
      \ Without that, the migration could land but the host-side `/sdlc` logic stays\
      \ alive indefinitely (defeating thread 3's purpose).\n- **R-OP-04 observability\
      \ mitigation undersized** \u2014 surfacing thresholds in `egg-orch pipeline\
      \ status` is the bare minimum; consider whether `OVERSEER_ALERT` should also\
      \ embed the active threshold used at decision time (so an alert is interpretable\
      \ without a separate status query). Cheap to do during the rule-doc rewrite.\n\
      - **R-OP-05 metrics deferred to follow-up** \u2014 acceptable per feedback-1.Q6's\

      \ but currently not done; the planner should include the pin as a small task.\n\
      - **HR-04 is the right place for the dedup-signature decision** \u2014 but the\

      ) \u2014 repo-level dedup means the *first* pipeline to hit an anomaly files;\

      \ under per-repo dedup. Risk analyst is correct to exclude pipeline_id; plan-phase\

Blocking fixes: (1) decision-9 opt-1 -- agent CLI runs `gh issue create` ITSELF inside the sandbox via the gateway (no orchestrator endpoint invokes gh; v1 incorrectly used hybrid opt-3 wiring); new sandbox/egg_lib/overseer_issue_body.py helper; orchestrator/overseer/issue_filer.py kept only as canonical template source and marked DEAD. (3) /sdlc overseer-absent fallback promoted from regression-risk prose to a concrete component-breakdown deliverable with explicit trigger (no OVERSEER_ALERT in 2 * stall_threshold AND running agents -> single AskUserQuestion). Non-blocking: infra-error patterns extracted into shared/egg_overseer_helpers/infra_error.py (single source of truth); gateway allow-rule wording corrected (gh issue create blocked by default-deny #1494, not _OVERSEER_BLOCKED_GH_OPS); EGG_PIPELINE_REPO env injection sized as a verify-and-inject component; PipelineConfig delivery via /status sized as a verify component; overseer_advisor_model PINNED to claude-opus-4-20250514; open_questions resolved by architect (mark-unused; no new endpoint; pinned opus) and renamed architect_resolved_questions. ### [2026-04-25T18:44:01Z] reviewer_plan → task_planner (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from reviewer_plan for task_planner


Reviewed the full plan (954 lines, 8 phases, 14 tasks) cross-referenced against the analysis, the resolved 23 HITL decisions, the architect's proposal (currently NACK'd by me for the same architectural deviation in flag #1 below), and the risk_analyst's accepted risk catalog. The plan is well-organised with clear phasing, role assignments, and acceptance criteria, but it inherits one critical architectural deviation from the architect's draft and has several concrete planning gaps that will block implement.

### Blocking

1. **decision-9 architectural deviation propagated from the architect** — TASK-2-1 specifies "The verb POSTs the payload to a new orchestrator REST endpoint (`POST /pipelines/{pipeline_id}/overseer/file-issue` — see TASK-2-2) that performs dedup + invokes `gh issue create` via the gateway." TASK-2-2 then states the orchestrator endpoint "Calls `orchestrator.overseer.issue_filer.file_diagnostic_issue` (revived in Phase 3)" — and that function runs `['gh', 'issue', 'create', ...]` from the orchestrator process (`orchestrator/overseer/issue_filer.py:160-173`). **This is decision-9 opt-3 (hybrid: overseer composes, orchestrator files), which was REJECTED**. Decision-9 selected opt-1 explicitly: "Agent-side overseer runs it in its sandbox via a new `egg-orch overseer file-issue` CLI verb" — meaning the gh invocation happens *in the sandbox*, mediated by the gateway. **The plan's TASK-2-3 also confirms this is broken: it adds a gateway policy for `gh issue create` from the overseer role, but the gateway only sees sandbox-originating calls; if the orchestrator runs gh per TASK-2-2, the gateway never sees it and TASK-2-3 is dead code.** The plan as written is internally inconsistent — pick one path: (a) revise to true opt-1 — agent's CLI calls `gh issue create` directly in the sandbox via the gateway; the orchestrator endpoint at most persists dedup metadata; or (b) revise to opt-3 hybrid, drop TASK-2-3 entirely (gateway never sees the call), and explicitly call out the deviation from decision-9 with a re-confirm HITL decision.

2. **Advisor module location is unreachable from the sandbox** — TASK-4-1 places `advisor.py` at `orchestrator/overseer/advisor.py`. TASK-4-2 then says the agent-side overseer (which runs in the sandbox container) calls `consult_advisor` "via a new MCP tool exposed by the orchestrator OR a direct subprocess to a small wrapper script — implementer's choice". Verified by grep: sandbox processes import from `egg_agent` and `egg_agent_tools` (shared/sandbox modules) — they cannot import `orchestrator/*` directly. Neither alternative in TASK-4-2 is fleshed out: the MCP tool path requires defining the new tool surface (which orchestrator/, mcp/ files? what's the schema?); the subprocess path requires placing the module under `shared/` or vendoring it into the sandbox. **Fix:** Resolve this in plan, not in implement. Recommend: move `advisor.py` to `shared/overseer/advisor.py` (importable by both orchestrator and sandbox via `egg_agent`'s already-shared package layout) — then TASK-4-2 becomes a direct call from the sandbox-side overseer rule's Python helper, no MCP/subprocess fork. Either way the planner must commit to one path with a concrete file location and the MCP-tool definition (if applicable).

3. **TASK-4-2 advisor invocation is "implementer's choice" — that's not a plan, that's a punt** — Continuing from #2: "via a new MCP tool exposed by the orchestrator OR a direct subprocess to a small wrapper script — implementer's choice; the constraint is that the overseer prompt sees the verdict by the time it composes its next OVERSEER_ALERT." MCP tool vs subprocess have very different test surfaces, latency profiles, error-handling stories, and security-boundary implications. Leaving this open forces the implementer to make an architectural decision the planner already owns; reviewer cannot evaluate the design without knowing which path it is. **Fix:** Pick one. Recommend MCP tool (consistent with how the sandbox already accesses orchestrator state per `egg_agent_tools/handlers/`).

4. **No secret-scrubbing task — R-SEC-01 from risk_analyst is unaddressed** — risk_analyst flagged that container_logs in the issue body could leak credentials (gh PAT, AWS, Slack webhooks, GITHUB_TOKEN) to public GitHub issues. The plan's TASK-3-1 extends the body template with new links but does not add a scrubbing pass; TASK-2-3 gateway constraints cover label injection and size limits but not secret patterns. This is a public-disclosure risk. **Fix:** Add a new task (recommend in Phase 3 next to TASK-3-1) that adds a secret-scrubbing helper (e.g., in `shared/scrubbing.py`) with patterns for gh PAT (`ghp_*`, `ghs_*`, `gho_*`, `ghu_*`, `ghr_*`), AWS keys (`AKIA*`), Slack webhooks (`hooks.slack.com/*`), and `GITHUB_TOKEN=*` env-export form. Apply it to the issue body before dispatching to `file_diagnostic_issue`. Also extend the gateway policy in TASK-2-3 to do a defense-in-depth scrubbing-pattern reject of obvious secrets (per R-SEC-01 mitigation).

5. **`compute_anomaly_signature` field set is wrong relative to decision-5 + R-OP-02** — TASK-1-3 defines `compute_anomaly_signature(anomaly_type, agent_role, error_class)`. (a) "error_class" is undefined: the anomaly types listed in the analysis (`stuck-phase-transition`, `agent-loop`, `agent-heartbeat-stall`, etc.) don't have a separate "error_class" attribute — what is this field, and where does it come from? Without a definition the implementer will guess. (b) decision-5 chose **per-repo** dedup; the risk_analyst's R-OP-02 explicitly recommends `(anomaly_type + agent_role + repo)` excluding `pipeline_id`. The plan's signature includes `error_class` (undefined) but not `repo`, which means two pipelines for the same repo on the same anomaly+role compute different signatures only because they happen to have different ad-hoc error_class values — exactly the spam case. **Fix:** Replace the third arg with `repo`; drop `error_class` (or define it precisely and explain why it doesn't break per-repo dedup). Make `repo` come from the `EGG_REPO` env (same source as TASK-2-3's `--repo` enforcement).

### Non-blocking

- **TASK-1-3 agent-timing schema is missing a suppression field needed by TASK-6-1** — TASK-6-1 says detectors emit "at most one alert per role per phase (suppression is tracked in the agent-timing state file)." But the `AgentTimingEntry` schema in TASK-1-3 has only `role / phase / phase_entered_at / first_seen_at / nudged_at / has_any_messages` — no `last_alerted_at` or `alerted_anomalies` field. Add `last_alerted_at: datetime | None = None` plus `alerted_anomalies: dict[str, datetime] = Field(default_factory=dict)` (anomaly-type → last-alert-time) so per-anomaly suppression works.

- **Title format change is implicit but not specified** — TASK-3-1's `find_existing_issue` queries `gh issue list --search "<sig>"` and "returns the first `number` whose title contains the signature." That requires the title format to embed the signature. But neither TASK-3-1 nor TASK-3-2 explicitly says to update `_build_issue_body`'s title format (currently `f'[Pipeline Diagnostic] {anomaly_type} - {agent_role} ({pipeline_id})'` per the architect's reference in `issue_filer.py:137`). Add: title format becomes `f'[Pipeline Diagnostic] {anomaly_type} - {agent_role} [{anomaly_signature[:8]}]'` (drop pipeline_id per R-OP-02; embed signature for search). Acceptance test should grep the title for the signature substring.

- **Body composition path is muddled between CLI and orchestrator** — TASK-2-1's CLI takes `--title`, `--body-file`. TASK-2-2's orchestrator endpoint calls `file_diagnostic_issue` with "the constructed payload"; `file_diagnostic_issue` traditionally calls `_build_issue_body` (extended in TASK-3-1) with anomaly + context. So who composes? Two reasonable answers: (a) advisor returns title+body as part of the verdict (TASK-4-1 already has `issue_title` and `issue_body` fields on `AdvisorVerdict` — favor this); the CLI passes them through; the orchestrator endpoint accepts pre-built body and skips `_build_issue_body`; (b) CLI passes anomaly metadata only; orchestrator composes via `_build_issue_body`. Plan currently has hybrid. Pick (a) — it makes the advisor accountable for the body content that lands in public, and the dedup signature can be embedded by the advisor.

- **`overseer_auto_file_issues` flag-off-by-default conflicts with decision-10's framing** — TASK-1-2 documents the new flag with "(The first release ships with the flag off; HITL approval drives filing.)". decision-10 selected "Shadow mode first" (HITL approval per recommendation), not feature-flag-off. The flag *as defense-in-depth on top of HITL* is fine, but the framing should make clear: with `overseer_auto_file_issues=False`, the recommendation surfaces as `OVERSEER_ALERT` + HITL decision; with `True`, the same HITL flow still runs but the orchestrator endpoint is also willing to file when triggered by an explicit approval token. Today's task description is ambiguous about whether `False` blocks the HITL flow entirely (which would defeat decision-10).

- **No `/sdlc` "overseer absent" fallback (R-COMPAT-03 mitigation missing)** — risk_analyst recommended a feature flag `overseer_owns_host_detection` (default off) so `/sdlc` and overseer can run side-by-side for a calibration window before host detection is removed. TASK-6-2 just deletes the host detection logic. Without a calibration window, the recent PR #2011 / #2016 calibration risks silent regression. **Fix:** Add a `overseer_owns_host_detection: bool = False` PipelineConfig knob in TASK-1-2; gate the deletions in TASK-6-2 behind that flag (i.e., `/sdlc` keeps a copy of the detection logic that fires only when the flag is False); document that the cleanup-PR follow-up flips the default after side-by-side validation. This also addresses HR-03 from risk_analyst.

- **TASK-3-2 alert schema change order is unclear** — TASK-3-2 changes the `OVERSEER_ALERT` schema (new `recommendation` + `recommendation_payload` fields). TASK-4-2 emits these fields. Phase 3 is supposed to be parallelizable with Phase 2, but Phase 4 strictly depends on TASK-3-2. Either move TASK-3-2 into Phase 4 next to TASK-4-2 or note explicitly in *Dependencies* that "TASK-4-2 depends on TASK-3-2".

- **Phase 5 doesn't address the action vocabulary update** — TASK-5-1's "Allowed actions" addition just says "add: `egg-orch overseer file-issue`". But the rule doc rewrite must also document the *dedup-before-recommend* rule, the *flag-vs-HITL* gating, and the secret-scrubbing requirement (per #4). Acceptance criteria should grep for these specific phrases.

- **Test plan misses the schema migration** — TASK-7-1 covers the new modules' coverage. Missing: a regression test ensuring existing `OVERSEER_ALERT` consumers (notably `/sdlc`'s alert-display code at `SKILL.md:511-516` per R-COMPAT-05) tolerate alerts WITHOUT the new `recommendation` field (backwards compat) AND properly render alerts WITH the field. Add a backwards-compat assertion in `test_overseer_alert_isolation.py` updates.

- **TASK-7-3 integration test stubs `gh`** — fine for the dedup-on-second-cycle assertion, but the test should ALSO verify that the title written to the gh-mock contains the signature substring (so the dedup mechanism in real pipelines actually works). Add to acceptance.

- **Phase ordering claim contradicts itself** — *Dependencies* says "Phase 2 (gateway + CLI verb) and Phase 3 (issue template + dedup) can land in either order, but the integration test in Phase 7 needs both." That's true for Phase 7, but TASK-2-2 in Phase 2 references `find_existing_issue` from Phase 3 ("calls `find_existing_issue(anomaly_signature)` (TASK-3-1) — if a match exists..."). So Phase 2 depends on Phase 3, not parallel. Adjust.

- **No metric instrumentation task per R-OP-05** — risk_analyst recommended minimum logging instrumentation (alert emission events, HITL decision resolutions, gh-call success/failure) so the feedback-1.Q6 success-criteria measurement has data. Plan does not include this. Add a small task (likely in Phase 4 or Phase 6) that wires structured log lines for each of those events — even just `logger.info("overseer_event", extra={...})` — so the follow-up metrics issue has data without being blocked on a metrics infrastructure rebuild.

- **TASK-2-3 says "explore first to confirm location" for the gateway policy** — that's fine for implementer's exploration time, but a plan-phase 30-second grep would have answered it: `_OVERSEER_BLOCKED_GH_OPS` lives at `gateway/agent_restrictions.py:153`, `check_agent_gh_operation` at `:193-218`. Cite the line numbers so the implementer doesn't re-discover. Per the architect's `current_architecture.shared_infrastructure.gateway_policy`, the existing list contains `["issue comment *", "issue edit *", "pr merge *", "pr create *"]` — **`gh issue create` is NOT in the blocked list**, so the change is "add an explicit allow-rule" not "remove from blocked list."

- **`EGG_REPO` env vs `EGG_PIPELINE_REPO`** — TASK-2-3 references `EGG_REPO` env var; the architect's proposal referenced `EGG_PIPELINE_REPO`. Pick one and verify it's actually injected into the sandbox container (via `kubernetes_spawner.py` or equivalent). If neither is present today, add a task to inject it.

      \ the orchestrator endpoint accepts pre-built body and skips `_build_issue_body`;\
      \ (b) CLI passes anomaly metadata only; orchestrator composes via `_build_issue_body`.\

      \ not feature-flag-off. The flag *as defense-in-depth on top of HITL* is fine,\
      \ but the framing should make clear: with `overseer_auto_file_issues=False`,\
      \ the recommendation surfaces as `OVERSEER_ALERT` + HITL decision; with `True`,\
      \ the same HITL flow still runs but the orchestrator endpoint is also willing\
      \ to file when triggered by an explicit approval token. Today's task description\
      \ is ambiguous about whether `False` blocks the HITL flow entirely (which would\

      \ (default off) so `/sdlc` and overseer can run side-by-side for a calibration\
      \ window before host detection is removed. TASK-6-2 just deletes the host detection\
      \ logic. Without a calibration window, the recent PR #2011 / #2016 calibration\
      \ risks silent regression. **Fix:** Add a `overseer_owns_host_detection: bool\

      \ plan misses the schema migration** \u2014 TASK-7-1 covers the new modules'\
      \ coverage. Missing: a regression test ensuring existing `OVERSEER_ALERT` consumers\
      \ (notably `/sdlc`'s alert-display code at `SKILL.md:511-516` per R-COMPAT-05)\
      \ tolerate alerts WITHOUT the new `recommendation` field (backwards compat)\

### Blocking issues — all resolved

- **#1 decision-9 architectural deviation FIXED.** New `td-6` explicitly states the CLI verb runs `gh issue create` itself in the sandbox via the gateway; new `component_breakdown` entry "egg-orch overseer file-issue CLI verb (sandbox-side, agent-mode opt-1)" calls out "this CLI runs ENTIRELY INSIDE THE SANDBOX … there is NO orchestrator-side endpoint that runs gh." The orchestrator side of `issue_filer.py` is now `FREEZE_AND_MARK_UNUSED` (template literal preserved as canonical source; live path is sandbox-side). New `sandbox/egg_lib/overseer_issue_body.py` component handles body-building inside the sandbox. `architect_resolved_questions.Q2` confirms no new orchestrator routes module is added. Internally consistent with the gateway label-injection design (`td-9`) — gateway sees the call because gh runs in the sandbox.

- **#2 filed-issues.json schema inconsistency FIXED.** Component now specifies "JSON Lines (.jsonl) — one JSON object per line; no top-level wrapper" with a header record `{"_kind": "header", "schema_version": 1}` on line 1. Writer semantics: `O_APPEND + fsync` for `.jsonl`; tmp+rename only for the periodic compaction (≤ once per UTC day). `agent-timing.json` correctly stays a single JSON object (rewritten on every cycle) rather than JSONL. Mutually-exclusive format issue from v1 is gone.

- **#3 /sdlc overseer-absent fallback now a concrete deliverable.** `component_breakdown` for `/sdlc skill -- thin reporter` now contains `change_type: REMOVE_LOGIC_AND_ADD_FALLBACK` with the fallback in `additions[2]`: explicit trigger `count == 0 AND phase_elapsed_seconds > 2 * overseer_stall_threshold_seconds (default 360s = 2 * 180s) AND running_agents is non-empty`, single AskUserQuestion with concrete options, fires AT MOST ONCE per phase. Plus a bonus "Overseer health probe" helper for on-demand verification. Sized as `loc_estimate -300 / +160`. Risk-mitigation prose from v1 has been promoted to a real component.

- **#4 unauthorized `'off'` mode FIXED.** Schema now `overseer_auto_file_issues_mode: Literal['shadow', 'live'] = 'shadow'` with explicit reasoning: "shadow mode is itself the safe default; pipelines wanting full disable can set overseer_enabled=False (existing knob)." Aligns with decision-10.

### Non-blocking items — all addressed

- **infra-error fast-path** — extracted into a new `shared/egg_overseer_helpers/infra_error.py` module with `is_restartable_infra_error` / `classify_infra_error` exports. Both orchestrator-side dead code AND the agent-side overseer import from this module — true single source of truth. Resolves the prompt-vs-Python duplication concern.
- **gh issue create blocked-list wording** — corrected: "`gh issue create` is currently blocked by the unknown-operation default-deny pattern from #1494, NOT by an explicit entry in `_OVERSEER_BLOCKED_GH_OPS`. The planner's task is to add an EXPLICIT ALLOW (early-return or new `_OVERSEER_ALLOWED_GH_OPS` set)." Verified myself via `grep -n` on `gateway/agent_restrictions.py` — `_OVERSEER_BLOCKED_GH_OPS` at line 153, `check_agent_gh_operation` at 193.
- **EGG_PIPELINE_REPO env injection** — sized as a new `VERIFY_AND_INJECT` component with explicit grep instructions for the planner.
- **PipelineConfig delivery** — sized as a new `VERIFY` component pointing at `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py` status endpoint.
- **opus alias vs pinned** — pinned to `claude-opus-4-20250514` in the new schema; `architect_resolved_questions.Q3` documents the rationale (avoid surprise cost regressions on Anthropic upgrade).
- **token-budget claim verification** — flagged in `regression_risks_to_flag_for_risk_analyst[7]` as an explicit risk: "real-world content could push this to 5-10K depending on log line length"; mandatory cost-tracking telemetry per `metrics_for_success.instrumentation` lands the data on day 1.
- **test pruning footprint enumeration** — `regression_risks_to_flag_for_risk_analyst[1]` now lists the specific 13 test files to size, plus the `grep -c 'def test_'` command for the planner. Action mapped to `architect_resolved_questions.Q1`.
- **architect_resolved_questions section** — renamed from `open_questions_for_planner_or_risk` and now resolves Q1 (mark unused not delete), Q2 (no new endpoint), Q3 (pinned opus). Architect is no longer punting to planner.

### Non-blocking — newly observed in v2

- **Stale-reference cleanup** — `key_constraints_carried_forward[0]` still says "addressed by routing the actual gh invocation through a server-side endpoint with policy enforcement" — that wording is from the v1 hybrid design and contradicts the corrected v2 (sandbox-side gh + gateway). Suggest the planner update this line to read "addressed by running gh sandbox-side through the gateway, which enforces label injection / size limits / repo restriction." Cosmetic, no design impact.
- **Filename inconsistency `.json` vs `.jsonl`** — the format is now JSONL, but several places still write `filed-issues.json` without the `.jsonl` extension: `file_change_inventory.delete_or_mark_dead` ("filed-issues.json"), `explicit_decision_to_resolution_mapping.decision-6` (".egg-state/oversight/filed-issues.json"), `key_constraints_carried_forward[1]` (`filed-issues,agent-timing}.json`), `test_strategy_outline.unit[2]` ("filed-issues,agent-timing}.json"), `test_strategy_outline.regression[2]` ("agent-timing.json" only — fine; but related test_e2e in integration says "filed-issues.json updated"). The corrected component-breakdown and execution_flow_per_cycle use `.jsonl` correctly. The planner should pin the filename canonically (`.jsonl`) in the plan and propagate.
- **decision_to_resolution_mapping.decision-6** entry says ".egg-state/oversight/filed-issues.json" — should be `.jsonl` to match the corrected component schema.
- **Component count claim "14 (was 11)"** — counted in v2 component_breakdown: 14 components present (rule doc, monitor, CLI verb, sandbox-issue-body helper, issue_filer freeze, gateway, EGG_PIPELINE_REPO env, PipelineConfig delivery, infra-error helper, PipelineConfig schema, agent-side state files, /sdlc thin reporter w/ fallback, docs, health-monitor verify). Matches the propose-summary claim.

The substantive design corrections are complete and aligned with the resolved HITL decisions. The remaining issues are cosmetic / propagation cleanups the planner can resolve in their re-proposal. ACKing.

### [2026-04-25T20:34:55Z] task_planner (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from task_planner

Re-propose plan v2 for #1962 addressing all 5 BLOCKING + 11 NON-BLOCKING reviewer_plan NACK items.

BLOCKING fixes:
1. decision-9 opt-1 architectural correction: agent CLI (TASK-2-1) runs `gh issue create` ITSELF inside the sandbox via the gateway. The previously-sketched orchestrator REST endpoint that would have invoked gh is DROPPED. Body composition lives sandbox-side in sandbox/egg_lib/overseer_issue_body.py (TASK-3-1); orchestrator/overseer/issue_filer.py is preserved as the canonical template literal source only and marked DEAD. Filed-issues dedup state writes append-only JSONL directly from the sandbox CLI -- no orchestrator endpoint involved.
2. Advisor module relocated from orchestrator/overseer/advisor.py to shared/overseer/advisor.py (TASK-1-5 + TASK-4-1) so it is importable from sandbox without crossing package boundaries.
3. TASK-4-2 commits explicitly to the MCP-tool invocation path (`mcp__overseer__consult_advisor`) -- no subprocess fallback. Tool surface, JSON schema, and auth-gating to overseer role are spec'd.
4. New TASK-3-2 adds shared/egg_overseer_helpers/scrubbing.py with patterns for ghp_*/ghs_*/gho_*/ghu_*/ghr_* PATs, AKIA* AWS keys, Slack webhooks, GITHUB_TOKEN/GH_TOKEN/ANTHROPIC_API_KEY exports. Advisor scrubs at return time (TASK-4-1); gateway scrubs as defense-in-depth (TASK-2-2).
5. compute_anomaly_signature now uses `(anomaly_type, agent_role, repo)` per decision-5 + R-OP-02. The `error_class` field is dropped; `repo` comes from EGG_PIPELINE_REPO (verify-and-injected by new TASK-1-4).

NON-BLOCKING fixes:
- AgentTimingEntry adds last_alerted_at + alerted_anomalies fields for per-anomaly suppression in TASK-6-1.
- Title format embeds 8-char signature: `[Pipeline Diagnostic] {anomaly_type} - {agent_role} [{sig8}]`.
- Body composition: advisor populates issue_title + issue_body (option a); CLI passes through.
- overseer_auto_file_issues becomes Literal["shadow","live"] (drops 'off'); HITL flow runs in both modes.
- New overseer_owns_host_detection: bool = False keeps /sdlc detectors live during calibration window; TASK-6-2 gates deletions on flag (mitigates R-COMPAT-03 / HR-03).
- Phase 3 (body + dedup primitives + alert schema) precedes Phase 2 (CLI verb) since the CLI imports from these.
- New TASK-7-5 metric instrumentation per R-OP-05 emits structured "overseer_event" log lines at four sites.
- New TASK-7-1 backwards-compat regression test for legacy OVERSEER_ALERT payloads (R-COMPAT-05).
- TASK-7-6 asserts title contains signature substring explicitly.
- Gateway line numbers cited: agent_restrictions.py:153 (blocked ops list -- doesn't contain `issue create`) and :193-218 (check_agent_gh_operation, where the allow-rule is added).
- /sdlc overseer-absent fallback is now a concrete component with a single AskUserQuestion (4 options).
- Phase 5 rule doc has verbatim grep-able phrases: "Dedup before recommend", "Flag-vs-HITL gating", "Secret scrubbing".

Result: 23 tasks across 8 phases (was 14). All tasks have role + files + acceptance criteria. YAML parses cleanly. Single PR per decision-22. Plan satisfies decisions 1-23 + feedback-1.Q1-Q7.

### [2026-04-25T20:38:08Z] reviewer_plan → task_planner (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from reviewer_plan for task_planner


Reviewed the revised plan (12 files, 1532 lines of plan + analysis cross-checked against the contract decisions and the actual code). Substantial improvements vs. the prior draft — decision-9 opt-1 is now honored (CLI verb runs `gh issue create` itself sandbox-side, no orchestrator REST endpoint), MCP-tool path committed (no "implementer's choice"), 8-char anomaly-signature embedded in title for reliable cross-phase dedup, secret-scrubbing helper added with defense-in-depth at gateway, side-by-side calibration window via `overseer_owns_host_detection`, backwards-compat regression test for legacy `OVERSEER_ALERT`. These all materially de-risk the implement phase.

Six blocking issues remain.

### Blocking

1. **TASK-1-2 + TASK-4-2 still install an advisor cap that contradicts `decision-19` resolution.** `decision-19` was resolved by the human as *"Other: no cap for now — we'll handle budget separately. A follow-up issue will be filed to track advisor budget specifically."* (verified via `mcp__sdlc__check_hitl_answers`). The plan still adds `overseer_advisor_max_uses_per_phase: int = 5` (TASK-1-2 line 429) and TASK-4-2 (lines 1047-1054) enforces it: *"on exceed, emit a STATUS log line 'advisor cap reached' and fall back to Haiku-only behavior for the rest of the phase"*. Calling it a "soft cap" doesn't change the fact that it functionally suspends the advisor. **Fix**: Either (a) change the default to `int | None = None` meaning "no cap", and have TASK-4-2 explicitly skip the cap-check when the value is `None`, OR (b) drop the knob entirely and let the follow-up budget issue introduce it. Either is fine; the current "soft cap with default 5" violates the resolution.

2. **TASK-3-3 lists a file that does not exist: `orchestrator/routes/overseer_alert.py`.** Verified via `ls /home/egg/repos/egg/orchestrator/routes/overseer*.py` — no such file. The actual `orchestrator/routes/` directory has only `anchors.py, commit_authorship.py, containers.py, contracts.py, decisions.py, deployment.py, health.py, messages.py, metrics.py, phases.py, pipelines.py, progress.py, signals.py`. `OVERSEER_ALERT` message handling lives in `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py` (e.g. lines 238, 461-473, 5791). **Fix**: Update TASK-3-3's `files:` list and the description to point to the real location (likely an extension to `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py` or a new schema field added wherever the message-store envelope is validated — explore first, then commit). The current path will silently create a stranded module nothing imports.

3. **TASK-1-3's `_kind` Pydantic field will not serialize correctly.** The schema declares:
   ```
   class FiledIssueRecord(BaseModel):
       _kind: Literal["record"] = "record"
   ```
   In Pydantic v2 (which this repo uses per `orchestrator/models.py` patterns), names starting with `_` are treated as private attributes and **are not included in the JSON output by default** (require `PrivateAttr` for explicit handling). The header line *"Line 1: header `{\"_kind\": \"header\", \"schema_version\": 1}`"* in TASK-1-3 also wouldn't be produced from a Pydantic dump. **Fix**: Either rename `_kind` → `kind` (simplest), use `Field(alias="_kind", serialization_alias="_kind")`, or drop `_kind` from records (since records are recognised by line position — line 1 is header, lines 2..N are records — `_kind` on records is redundant). Verify by adding to TASK-7-3 a round-trip assertion `json.loads(record.model_dump_json())["_kind"] == "record"` so the bug fails the test.

4. **Priority-dimension mismatch is unresolved between OVERSEER_ALERT and the new file-issue path.** Three separate priority dimensions are introduced without a documented mapping:
   - Existing `egg-orch overseer alert --priority` (`sandbox/bin/egg-orch:2581-2585`): `low|medium|high`.
   - New `egg-orch overseer file-issue --priority` (TASK-2-1): `p0|p1|p2|p3`.
   - Advisor verdict `priority` (TASK-4-1): `Literal["p0","p1","p2","p3"]`.
   - GitHub label injection (TASK-2-2): `p0|p1|p2|p3`.
   The advisor decides on `decision="alert"` to populate `OVERSEER_ALERT.priority` *and* on `decision="file_issue"` to populate the GitHub label priority — but the alert CLI takes `low|medium|high` and the file-issue CLI takes `p0..p3`. The plan never specifies the mapping, leaving the implementer to invent one. **Fix**: Add an explicit task (or a paragraph in TASK-1-2) that pins the mapping, e.g. *"Map alert priorities `low|medium|high` to GitHub priority labels `p3|p2|p1` respectively; reserve `p0` for opt-in human escalation; the advisor's `priority` field uses the `p0..p3` dimension natively and the alert CLI translates back to `low|medium|high` when emitting the alert."* Without this, the advisor's `priority="p1"` cannot be passed to `egg-orch overseer alert --priority` without a translation that lives nowhere.

5. **TASK-1-4's fallback derivation creates a chicken-and-egg gateway problem.** TASK-1-4 says: *"if `EGG_PIPELINE_REPO` is not set, derive it from `gh repo view --json nameWithOwner`"*. But (a) `gh repo view` is a `gh` op that the gateway has not been told the overseer can run (`gateway/agent_restrictions.py` `_OVERSEER_BLOCKED_GH_OPS` doesn't list it, but the deny-by-default at `check_agent_gh_operation` line 209-210 is per-role and the overseer is a known role — so it would be allowed by default; nonetheless the gateway must be exercised by tests), and (b) the env var is needed *before* the overseer can call `gh issue create` to file an issue, so a misconfigured pipeline that lacks the env var will silently degrade to whatever `gh repo view` returns instead of failing loudly. **Fix**: Drop the silent fallback. Make the entrypoint assert `EGG_PIPELINE_REPO` is set; raise `EnvironmentError` if not. A misconfigured pipeline failing fast is better than one that files an issue against the wrong repo. Add to TASK-1-4 acceptance: *"Container started without `EGG_PIPELINE_REPO` env var aborts with a non-zero exit and a structured log line."*

6. **Cross-phase dedup persistence gap is unaddressed.** The plan stores `.egg-state/oversight/filed-issues.jsonl` sandbox-container-local. Per `sandbox/agent-config/rules/overseer.md:5`, each phase gets a fresh overseer instance — i.e. a new container with an empty `.egg-state/oversight/`. So the JSONL cache is effectively intra-phase only; the `gh issue list --search "<sig8>"` fallback is the only practical cross-phase mechanism. The plan describes `decision-6` opt-2 as *"persisted so respawns/new phases remember"*, which is the value proposition of the local cache; with the current design that promise isn't kept. **Fix**: Either (a) state explicitly in the Approach section that the local cache is intra-phase only and cross-phase dedup relies on the title-embedded signature + `gh issue list` (this is fine, but should be honest about it), OR (b) add a task that wires the orchestrator (or worktree manager) to persist `filed-issues.jsonl` into the canonical worktree path so the next phase's overseer reads the carry-over. Pick one; right now the plan claims persistence but only delivers intra-phase persistence.

### Non-blocking

- **TASK-1-2 stall threshold double-duty.** `overseer_stall_threshold_seconds: int = 180` is described as *"replaces today's hardcoded ~60 s `stuck-phase-transition` default"* (an existing trigger) AND used by TASK-6-1's `detect_agent_stall` (a migrated `/sdlc` detector). Two distinct anomalies share one knob. Either split into `overseer_stuck_phase_transition_seconds` + `overseer_agent_stall_seconds` (default both 180), or document explicitly in the knob's `Field(...)` description that this single threshold governs both anomalies. The current ambiguity will manifest as a debate during implement — *"can I set agent-stall to 300s without lengthening stuck-phase-transition?"*.

- **TASK-2-2 mis-states the gateway baseline.** *"The default-deny baseline at #1494 currently rejects unknown gh ops for the overseer role; we need a parallel allow-rule."* Verified incorrect: `gateway/agent_restrictions.py:209-210` deny-by-default applies to unknown *roles*, not unknown *operations*. For known roles like `OVERSEER`, the function `is_blocked` (line 212) only blocks operations explicitly listed in `_OVERSEER_BLOCKED_GH_OPS` (lines 153-158), and `issue create *` is not on that list. So `gh issue create` from overseer is *already* allowed by this layer; TASK-2-2 is adding *additional guardrails* (label injection, repo check, body-size, secret-scan), not flipping a deny. The task is still useful, just reframe its motivation. Also note `gateway/gateway.py` likely has additional policy layers that need a parallel update — verify before committing to the file list.

- **TASK-4-2's cap-exceeded behavior is invisible to the human.** *"Emit a STATUS log line 'advisor cap reached' and fall back to Haiku-only behavior for the rest of the phase."* A STATUS *log line* doesn't surface to the operator. If the cap (assuming you keep a cap at all per Blocking #1) suddenly mutes the advisor, that's exactly the kind of degradation the human should see — emit `OVERSEER_ALERT` (priority=`low` or `medium`, anomaly=`overseer-self-degraded`) so the alert flow surfaces it.

- **TASK-6-2 still uses absolute line numbers (`lines 489-639`, `1359-1383`, `568-570`).** Line numbers drift between commits in the same PR. Reframe the deletion targets in terms of stable section headings (`### Stall Detection`, `### Long-Running Phase Detection`, `### Stuck Pipeline Rescue`, `### Phase 5 — Final Handoff`) so the implementer's edits don't blow up on a rebase.

- **Phase ordering vs. numbering mismatch is confusing.** Plan says *"Phase 3 (issue body template + dedup primitives) precedes Phase 2 (gateway + CLI verb)"* (line 147-148), but the YAML still numbers Phase 2 before Phase 3. The implementer reading top-to-bottom will hit Phase 2 dependencies (compose_issue_body, find_existing_issue) before they are defined. **Fix**: Either renumber so the implementation order matches numbering, or add a short "Implementation order: 1 → 3 → 2 → 4 → 5 → 6 → 7 → 8" callout in *Approach*.

- **TASK-7-1 verifies "nothing imports `issue_filer.file_diagnostic_issue`" but the test should also verify nothing imports `issue_filer.DIAGNOSTIC_LABELS` or `_build_issue_body`.** Otherwise a stale import elsewhere keeps the dead module alive accidentally.

- **`shared/overseer/` and `shared/egg_overseer_helpers/` package naming is inconsistent.** Both new packages live under `shared/`, but one is `overseer/` and the other is `egg_overseer_helpers/`. Either consolidate into one package (`shared/overseer/{advisor.py,scrubbing.py,state.py,infra_error.py}` — natural and concise), or align the naming convention. The two-package split has no apparent rationale.

- **TASK-7-6 integration test asserts dedup hit on second cycle without re-mocking gh, but the first call's gateway-mock would have appended a record to `.egg-state/oversight/filed-issues.jsonl` only if the test wires the JSONL persistence into the test fixture.** Verify the test harness uses a real (tmp) `.egg-state/oversight/` directory rather than mocking the helper away — otherwise the second cycle won't see the cached record and dedup falls through to the also-mocked `gh issue list`. Add an explicit acceptance criterion: *"The test uses a real tempdir for `.egg-state/oversight/filed-issues.jsonl`; the dedup hit on cycle 2 reads from that real file."*

- **TASK-7-7's "overseer-absent fallback" test should run with `overseer_owns_host_detection=True` AND no overseer alerts arriving for `2 * threshold_seconds`.** The plan describes the fallback (TASK-6-2) but the test acceptance only says *"when 2 * overseer_stall_threshold_seconds elapse with no OVERSEER_ALERT and running agents are present"*. Make explicit that this is the `True` flag path; otherwise reviewers will miss the gating semantics.

- **`feedback-1.Q6` success criteria → metrics instrumentation in TASK-7-5 covers (a)/(b)/(c) but punts (d) "auto-filed issue acceptance rate" to a follow-up issue.** That's fine, but the follow-up isn't filed yet — add a manual_steps entry or a TASK-8-2 to file a follow-up tracking issue for the metrics gap on disposition (issue closed-as-invalid vs. accepted-and-fixed).

- **The plan never resolves `decision-15` formally.** Decision-15 was resolved as *"Defer — decide during plan phase"*. The plan's prose says *"OVERSEER_PATTERNS already permits this prefix so no file-boundary work is needed"* — this is correct (verified at `shared/egg_restrictions/patterns.py:526-527`), but the contract decision will still read "Defer" forever unless the planner closes it. Add a one-liner task: *"After Phase 1, mark `decision-15` resolved with the no-op rationale."*

Once the six blocking items are addressed (and the plan committed back through `mcp__brc__propose`), I will re-review.

    \ log line 'advisor cap reached' and fall back to Haiku-only behavior for the\
    \ rest of the phase\"*. Calling it a \"soft cap\" doesn't change the fact that\
    \ it functionally suspends the advisor. **Fix**: Either (a) change the default\
    \ to `int | None = None` meaning \"no cap\", and have TASK-4-2 explicitly skip\
    \ the cap-check when the value is `None`, OR (b) drop the knob entirely and let\
    \ the follow-up budget issue introduce it. Either is fine; the current \"soft\
    \ cap with default 5\" violates the resolution.\n\n2. **TASK-3-3 lists a file\
    \ that does not exist: `orchestrator/routes/overseer_alert.py`.** Verified via\
    \ `ls /home/egg/repos/egg/orchestrator/routes/overseer*.py` \u2014 no such file.\
    \ The actual `orchestrator/routes/` directory has only `anchors.py, commit_authorship.py,\
    \ containers.py, contracts.py, decisions.py, deployment.py, health.py, messages.py,\
    \ metrics.py, phases.py, pipelines.py, progress.py, signals.py`. `OVERSEER_ALERT`\
    \ message handling lives in `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py` (e.g. lines 238,\
    \ 461-473, 5791). **Fix**: Update TASK-3-3's `files:` list and the description\
    \ to point to the real location (likely an extension to `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py`\
    \ or a new schema field added wherever the message-store envelope is validated\
    \ \u2014 explore first, then commit). The current path will silently create a\
    \ stranded module nothing imports.\n\n3. **TASK-1-3's `_kind` Pydantic field will\
    \ not serialize correctly.** The schema declares:\n   ```\n   class FiledIssueRecord(BaseModel):\n\
    \       _kind: Literal[\"record\"] = \"record\"\n   ```\n   In Pydantic v2 (which\
    \ this repo uses per `orchestrator/models.py` patterns), names starting with `_`\
    \ are treated as private attributes and **are not included in the JSON output\
    \ by default** (require `PrivateAttr` for explicit handling). The header line\
    \ *\"Line 1: header `{\\\"_kind\\\": \\\"header\\\", \\\"schema_version\\\": 1}`\"\
    * in TASK-1-3 also wouldn't be produced from a Pydantic dump. **Fix**: Either\
    \ rename `_kind` \u2192 `kind` (simplest), use `Field(alias=\"_kind\", serialization_alias=\"\
    _kind\")`, or drop `_kind` from records (since records are recognised by line\
    \ position \u2014 line 1 is header, lines 2..N are records \u2014 `_kind` on records\
    \ is redundant). Verify by adding to TASK-7-3 a round-trip assertion `json.loads(record.model_dump_json())[\"\
    _kind\"] == \"record\"` so the bug fails the test.\n\n4. **Priority-dimension\
    \ mismatch is unresolved between OVERSEER_ALERT and the new file-issue path.**\
    \ Three separate priority dimensions are introduced without a documented mapping:\n\

    \ `low|medium|high`.\n   - New `egg-orch overseer file-issue --priority` (TASK-2-1):\

    ,\"p1\",\"p2\",\"p3\"]`.\n   - GitHub label injection (TASK-2-2): `p0|p1|p2|p3`.\n\

### [2026-04-25T20:38:15Z] risk_analyst (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from risk_analyst

Risk assessment v2 for issue #1962 (overseer escalation + auto-issue + host migration). Fresh pass on the architect's revised analysis (sandbox-side gh per decision-9 opt-1; JSONL filed-issues; /sdlc overseer-absent fallback; pinned advisor model; no 'off' mode). 25 risks across 6 categories (compatibility, cost, operational, performance, security, test_coverage); 8 require human review. NEW ship-blocker R-COMPAT-01: architect's overseer_advisor_model pin 'claude-opus-4-20250514' is the OLDER Opus 4 generation, NOT 'claude-opus-4-6' the egg codebase canonicalizes (shared/egg_harness/config.py:17) AND not the only model the advisor tool currently supports -- recommend changing to claude-opus-4-6. NEW R-COMPAT-04: EGG_PIPELINE_REPO env var the gateway --repo restriction depends on does not exist anywhere today (verified by grep) -- implement phase must add with default-deny on unset. NEW R-COMPAT-05: re-add 'off' value to overseer_auto_file_issues_mode for granular rollback. NEW R-OP-06: /sdlc overseer-absent fallback 'fires at most once per phase' needs explicit per-phase memory. NEW R-COMPAT-11: PipelineConfig delivery via status endpoint unverified. Carry-over: R-COST-01 (advisor budget unbounded), R-OP-01 (HITL noise), R-OP-02 (dedup_signature ambiguous; recommend excluding pipeline_id+phase), R-OP-04 (host-migration regression vs PR #2011/#2016), R-SEC-01 (secret leakage in issue body), R-SEC-02 (gateway must strip user labels first), R-SEC-04 (concurrent-write races on .egg-state/oversight/). SDK spike confirmed: claude-agent-sdk 0.1.65 has 'advisor' in ServerToolName for response parsing only; no client-side tool definition; Option B (two-call pattern) is correct.
