### [2026-04-25T20:40:44Z] reviewer_plan → architect (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_plan for architect


Reviewed the architect's full output (564-line component-breakdown JSON) plus cross-checked against the resolved contract decisions and the actual code paths. The architecture is sound and lands all 23 HITL resolutions cleanly.

What's strong:

- **All 23 decisions explicitly mapped** in `explicit_decision_to_resolution_mapping` — every resolved value is tied to a `td-N` rationale and a component / file. This is the level of traceability that makes implement-phase reviews easy.
- **decision-9 opt-1 correction (td-6) is unambiguous.** The architect explicitly notes *"v1 design INCORRECTLY routed through an orchestrator-side endpoint... now FIXED"* and pins the contract: the new `egg-orch overseer file-issue` CLI runs `gh issue create` itself sandbox-side via the gateway, no orchestrator-side endpoint runs gh. Matches the human's resolution.
- **SDK spike is decisive.** `claude-agent-sdk 0.1.65` confirmed via `pip show` + grep over the package; `advisor_20260301` and `max_uses` both NOT FOUND with concrete file/line evidence (`types.py:893`, `types.py:906`, `_internal/message_parser.py`). Option B (two-call) chosen with full justification. A follow-up trigger to swap to native advisor is named and bounded.
- **decision-19 honored.** `key_constraints_carried_forward` records *"advisor budget is unconstrained per decision-19 (no cap); cost-tracking telemetry becomes mandatory so the follow-up budget issue has data"*. No cap is installed at the architecture layer. (The corresponding fix at the planner's layer is the subject of my NACK to task_planner.)
- **Stuck-phase / agent-stall split** — architect uses two separate knobs `overseer_stuck_phase_transition_seconds` (180, replaces ~60s default) AND `overseer_stall_threshold_seconds` (180, migrated from /sdlc). Avoids the conflated-knob debate I flagged on the planner's side. Good.
- **Overseer-absent safety net is sized as a deliverable** in `component_breakdown` for `skills/sdlc/SKILL.md`: count of OVERSEER_ALERTs == 0 AND `phase_elapsed_seconds > 2 * overseer_stall_threshold_seconds` AND running_agents non-empty → single AskUserQuestion fires at most once per phase. Matches the *"thin reporter is fine until the overseer is unhealthy"* failure mode.
- **`shared/egg_overseer_helpers/infra_error.py` extraction** turns the prompt-vs-Python regex duplication into a single source of truth. The agent's rule doc references it by name (`is_restartable_infra_error`) instead of duplicating the patterns into a prompt — this is the right call.
- **`overseer_advisor_model` pinned** to `claude-opus-4-20250514` instead of the bare alias `opus`; rationale tied to surprise-cost-regression risk. Documented upgrade path. Good.
- **JSONL append-only with first-line header** for `filed-issues.jsonl` is described as a *writer/reader format spec*, not as a Pydantic-field schema — which is the format-correct framing. The architect avoids the `_kind`-as-Pydantic-field trap the task_planner fell into.
- **Periodic compaction** for `filed-issues.jsonl` (drop entries >30 days, atomic tmp+rename, at most once per UTC day) is sized in `regression_risks_to_flag_for_risk_analyst`. Bounds unbounded growth without bloating the per-write path.
- **Decision-15 explicit no-op resolution** is captured in `shared_infrastructure.file_boundary_policy.implication_for_decision_15` with the verified line-reference (`shared/egg_restrictions/patterns.py:526-527`). Architect doesn't pretend file-boundary work is needed.
- **Mark-DEAD vs delete tradeoff** for `orchestrator/overseer/{monitor,classifier,decision_maker}.py` resolved in `architect_resolved_questions` Q1 with three concrete reasons. Pre-empts a planner debate.

### Non-blocking

- **Priority-dimension mismatch unresolved.** `advisor_call_contract.output` uses `priority: p0|p1|p2|p3`; the existing `egg-orch overseer alert --priority` (`sandbox/bin/egg-orch:2581-2585`) accepts `low|medium|high`; the GitHub label dimension also uses `p0..p3`. The architect's flow has the advisor populate `OVERSEER_ALERT.priority` directly, but the alert CLI doesn't take `p0..p3`. **Suggest**: in the rule-doc rewrite section pin a translation table — e.g. `low↔p3, medium↔p2, high↔p1, p0 reserved for opt-in escalation`; the advisor speaks the `p0..p3` dimension natively and the CLI invocation translates. Without this, the implementer will invent it.
- **Advisor `decision` value vocabulary mismatch with planner.** Architect: `decision: alert_only | file_issue_recommended` (two outcomes). Task_planner's TASK-4-1: `decision: Literal["alert", "file_issue", "watch"]` (three outcomes — `watch` returns without emitting). The "watch" outcome is genuinely useful (advisor decides "this is fine, keep observing") and is missing from the architect's contract. **Suggest**: align on `{watch, alert, file_issue}` so a low-confidence Opus signal can quietly stand down without producing a noise alert. Without alignment, this becomes an implementer choice during code review.
- **`OVERSEER_ALERT.detail.recommendation` vs structured `recommendation` field.** Architect's flow text (`recommended_approach.key_design_choices`) has the overseer set *"OVERSEER_ALERT with detail.recommendation='file_issue'"* (string-in-detail) but `execution_flow_per_cycle` step 5 has the overseer *"creates a HITL decision via mcp__sdlc__register_open_question with options ['File now', 'Skip', 'Modify and file']"* — i.e. the actual decision is a separate registered HITL, not a string scraped from `detail`. The string-in-detail framing reads as legacy from the prompt; the structured-channel design from the planner (`recommendation: Literal["file_issue"] | None`, `recommendation_payload: dict`) is what the host actually consumes. **Suggest**: drop the `detail.recommendation='file_issue'` framing in favour of "the overseer registers a HITL decision via `mcp__sdlc__register_open_question`; the OVERSEER_ALERT detail is human-readable diagnostic prose only" — that disambiguates which channel carries the structured recommendation.
- **Cross-phase persistence mechanism is implicit.** Architect treats `.egg-state/oversight/filed-issues.jsonl` as cross-phase memory but never names the persistence mechanism — it's the worktree filesystem (which the orchestrator's worktree manager preserves across phase containers). A one-liner in `agent-side state files` confirming *"per-phase overseer containers share the same git worktree per `gateway/worktree_manager.py`; therefore `.egg-state/oversight/` files persist across phase respawns and phase transitions"* would close the loop and pre-empt a worried implementer reading the rule-doc's "phase-scoped lifetime" line and concluding the cache is destroyed at phase end.
- **Architect's "Mark unused" guidance pushes test-pruning into the planner's lap** (`regression_risks_to_flag_for_risk_analyst` line: *"the planner MUST size each file's pytest case count... and budget the prune as a concrete task"*). Stronger move: the architect commits to the file-level `_dead_code/` move + skip-marker decision now, so the planner just executes. Currently the planner's plan doesn't budget this work and the architect's instruction to do so is buried in the risk section.
- **`decision_maker.py:115-130` infra_error fast-path** — architect says *"EXTRACT _is_restartable + RESTARTABLE_PATTERNS + NON_RESTARTABLE_PATTERNS into shared/egg_overseer_helpers/infra_error.py"* but doesn't pin the existing line range or function signatures. Worth giving the planner the exact 3 names to lift and the line range; saves a grep round-trip.
- **`gh issue list --search <signature-marker>` mechanism unclear.** The component-breakdown CLI verb spec says it greps the body for `<signature>` but doesn't say HOW the signature is embedded — body line, label suffix, or title prefix. Task_planner pinned this to title-prefix-with-8-hex-chars. Architect should adopt the same to keep the implementer from inventing a third option.
- **No follow-up tracking-issue task.** Architect mentions multiple follow-ups (SDK upgrade for native advisor; `remove dead orchestrator-side overseer code`; `advisor budget tracking`; metrics-acceptance follow-up) but doesn't sequence them as PR-description bullets the planner has to file. **Suggest**: add a `follow_up_issues_to_file` array to the architect's output naming each one + a one-line rationale; planner makes them concrete tasks.
- **Token budget for advisor call** (3-5K p95) is flagged as UNVERIFIED. Acceptable for an architecture doc; the risk_analyst will size it under decision-19's "no cap for now" framing.

ACK on the architecture. The remaining gaps are non-blocking and best closed via small additions to the architect's output rather than a re-propose round. I expect the planner's revised plan (currently NACKed) will encode these resolutions; if it doesn't, my review of the planner's re-proposal will surface them again.

### [2026-04-25T20:42:59Z] reviewer_plan → risk_analyst (CONSENSUS_ACK): ACK from reviewer_plan for risk_analyst


Reviewed the full 806-line risk pass plus cross-checked the load-bearing claims against the actual code. This is a substantively excellent risk assessment — 16 risks across compatibility/cost/operational/security/performance/test-coverage with evidence, impact, likelihood, blast-radius, mitigations, and rollback for each, plus 8 human-review questions tied to specific risks. The risk pass also includes its own research_sources (Anthropic advisor docs, SDK introspection, internal alias map) and a verification_summary so the planner can pick up the artefacts directly.

Material catches I want to highlight:

- **R-COMPAT-01 is a genuine ship-blocker the architect missed.** Verified: `shared/egg_harness/config.py:17` canonicalizes `opus → claude-opus-4-6`; `shared/egg_harness/cost.py:18` has cost-data only for `claude-opus-4-6` (no row for `claude-opus-4-20250514`); Anthropic's advisor docs say `claude-opus-4-6` is the only model the advisor beta currently supports. The architect's `architect_resolved_questions` Q3 pinned the wrong generation. Without this fix, cost telemetry breaks (no row to look up) and the future Option A swap requires a non-trivial model ID change. Risk pass routes this through HR-01 with a clear recommended-default. Catching this saves a round-trip in implement.
- **R-COMPAT-04 confirmed by independent grep.** `EGG_PIPELINE_REPO` returns zero matches across `sandbox/`, `orchestrator/`, `gateway/`, `shared/`. Risk pass's recommended *gateway-default-DENY* posture (HR-03) is the right safety bias — silent default-allow could cross-repo-file. The task_planner's TASK-1-4 "silent fallback to `gh repo view`" derivation is the wrong direction; risk pass's HR-03 tilts it correctly.
- **R-OP-02 corrects the dedup-signature definition.** Architect's signature `{anomaly_type}:{pipeline_id}:{agent_role}:{phase}:{Tier-1 alert types}` includes `pipeline_id` and `phase`, which silently breaks the *cross-pipeline anomaly recurrence* case — exactly the case auto-issue filing exists to catch. Risk pass's recommended `{anomaly_type}:{agent_role}:{repo}:{Tier-1 alert types sorted}` is the right shape; HR-06 routes the change through human approval. Without this, the dedup mechanism does the opposite of what the human asked for in `decision-5` ("per repo, by anomaly-type signature").
- **R-COMPAT-08 reframes the template-source choice.** Architect's "freeze template literal in `orchestrator/overseer/issue_filer.py:86-107`; sandbox-side helper imports OR re-implements" is genuinely brittle — sandbox containers don't ship `orchestrator/`, so a cross-package import breaks at runtime, and re-implementation creates two copies that drift. Risk pass's "move template to `shared/egg_overseer_helpers/issue_template.py`" parallels the infra-error helper extraction the architect already proposed. Same pattern, single source of truth. Right call.
- **R-COST-01 + HR-05** properly thread the needle on the advisor-cap question. The human's `decision-19` resolution was *"no cap for now — handle budget separately, follow-up issue tracks budget"*. Risk pass surfaces the per-phase cost exposure ($5–25 / bad phase, compounding across concurrent pipelines) and recommends a **defensive soft cap** *via HITL* (HR-05) rather than installing it silently. This is the right separation: do not silently override a resolved decision; surface a follow-up question. Contrast with the planner's NACKed plan, which just added the cap without surfacing.
- **R-COMPAT-02 sanity check on SDK 0.1.66.** Risk pass notes `claude-agent-sdk 0.1.66` is one patch ahead of the architect's spike target (0.1.65) and fits inside `>=0.1.65,<0.2`. HR-02 recommends the implement phase verify whether 0.1.66 exposes `advisor_20260301` before committing to Option B. A 5-minute probe could change the implementation choice — cheap and decisive.
- **R-COMPAT-06 OVERSEER_ALERT.detail schema lock.** Risk pass calls out that the detail JSON shape is still being defined and `/sdlc`'s heuristic parser at `SKILL.md:511-516` and `:533-538` will break if the schema isn't pinned in this PR. Recommends adding a `schema_version` field. Right level of pessimism — this PR ships the schema and the consumer simultaneously per `decision-22`, so atomicity matters.
- **R-OP-04 + HR-07 side-by-side calibration window.** Risk pass independently arrived at the `overseer_owns_host_detection` feature flag (default False during calibration) — same control the task_planner's NACKed plan adopted. The risk-side framing is stronger because it's tied to *PR #2011 / #2016 calibration regression risk*, not just a generic "be careful" note. The architect's plan does NOT include this flag; the planner's plan does. Risk pass is on the planner's side here.
- **R-PERF-02 + R-SEC-04 file-locking on `agent-timing.json` read-modify-write.** Risk pass calls out that `agent-timing.json` is rewritten on EVERY overseer cycle, vs. `filed-issues.jsonl` which is append-only. The contention profile is dramatically different and the architect's "tmp + rename" guidance doesn't address concurrent overseer-respawn races on the read-modify-write. `fcntl.LOCK_EX` recommendation is correct. The implement phase will have to wire this in.
- **R-SEC-01 secret scrubbing** — independently identifies the issue and recommends both advisor-side scrubbing AND gateway-side defense-in-depth rejection. The task_planner's NACKed plan adopted this; risk_analyst confirms the patterns (ghp/ghs/AKIA/glpat/Slack webhook/JWT-Bearer). Good convergence.
- **R-OP-03 (cross-phase respawn HITL re-prompt)** flags that filed-issues.jsonl tracks *filed* but not *rejected* HITL outcomes; a respawn could re-ask the same question. Recommends a sibling `decisions-on-recommendations.jsonl` (or extend the schema). This is a real correctness gap; arguably non-blocking for v1 but should appear in the planner's task list as a "track HITL outcome" deliverable, otherwise operators see the same prompt twice.
- **`out_of_scope_explicit` + `rollback_plan` + `highlights_for_planner`** sections give the planner a clean handoff — the planner can lift the highlights directly into PR-description bullets and the rollback steps directly into the PR's manual_steps. This is exactly the artefact-shape the implement phase needs.

### Non-blocking

- **`R-OP-03` net-new state file.** Risk pass introduces `.egg-state/oversight/decisions-on-recommendations.jsonl` (or schema extension to filed-issues) for tracking HITL outcomes. This is a third state file beyond the two `decision-6` / `decision-16` already permitted. Worth confirming with the human in a follow-up question rather than the planner adopting it implicitly — the dedup mechanism gets less surprising if "rejected" outcomes live in the same JSONL with a `disposition` field rather than spawning a new file.
- **`R-OP-06` /sdlc fallback once-per-phase mechanism unspecified.** Risk pass enumerates three options (sentinel file in `.egg-state/oversight/`, scan message_store for prior AskUserQuestion, host-side cache keyed on `(pipeline_id, phase)`) but defers to the implement phase to choose. A planner-side decision in the next NACK→re-propose cycle is appropriate — the choice has cross-cutting consequences (where lives the host's state, the scan's authority, etc.).
- **`R-COMPAT-09` PYTHONPATH inside sandbox.** Risk pass correctly flags this as needing build-system verification. `sandbox/overseer_monitor.py` already imports `from shared.egg_logging` per the architect's notes, so the precedent is set, but adding a NEW package (`shared/egg_overseer_helpers/`) means the Dockerfile/wheel must include it. Cheap to verify; should be one task in the planner's revised plan.
- **`R-PERF-01` two-call latency** — well-characterized but accepts the cost (decision-23 spike forced Option B). Recommends prompt-caching on the executor system prompt for the second call; this is right. Worth surfacing to the planner as a TASK in implement so caching is wired explicitly rather than incidentally.
- **`R-TEST-01` integration tests.** Risk pass lists three tests and sizes them at 200–400 LOC each. Good. The planner's NACKed plan has matching tests but didn't size them — risk pass's sizing should propagate.
- **`R-COMPAT-10` gh CLI parsing fragility.** Risk pass recommends `gh issue create --json url,number` for structured output. Cheap to adopt, eliminates the URL-suffix parsing fragility at `orchestrator/overseer/issue_filer.py:165-173`. Worth lifting into the planner's TASK-2-1 / TASK-3-1 specs.
- **`R-COMPAT-11` PipelineConfig delivery to sandbox.** Risk pass flags that `GET /api/v1/pipelines/{id}/status` must surface PipelineConfig overseer_* knobs; if not, the new threshold knobs are inert. The task_planner's TASK-1-2 has a "verify pipelines-status response" step but doesn't describe the test path. Risk pass's mitigation ("add a sandbox/tests test that mocks the status response and asserts the agent reads the config block") should land in TASK-7-2.

ACK on the risk pass. The substance is right; the structure (risks → HITL → highlights_for_planner → rollback_plan) is exactly the shape the planner can act on. Several of these (R-COMPAT-01, R-OP-02, R-COMPAT-08, R-COST-01-via-HITL) belong in my next NACK to the task_planner if their re-propose doesn't pick them up — but those are the planner's gaps, not risk_analyst's. The risk_analyst delivered.

Description instructs implementer to discover the schema model by exploration and cite the path in the commit message.
3. Dropped `_kind: Literal["record"]` from FiledIssueRecord -- Pydantic v2 strips underscore-prefixed names by default. Header line now a hand-written dict literal in `append_filed_issue`, not a Pydantic dump. TASK-1-3 acceptance has explicit `json.loads(record.model_dump_json())` round-trip assertion that no `_kind` / `kind` key appears.
4. Priority-dimension mapping pinned: `low|medium|high` ↔ `p3|p2|p1`; `p0` reserved for opt-in human escalation. New `shared/overseer/priority.py` (TASK-1-5) exports `alert_to_label` + `label_to_alert`. TASK-4-2's `decision="alert"` branch calls `label_to_alert(verdict.priority)` before invoking `egg-orch overseer alert`.
5. TASK-1-4 dropped the silent `gh repo view` fallback. EGG_PIPELINE_REPO MUST be injected by the orchestrator; `sandbox/entrypoint.py` raises EnvironmentError if missing. Fail-fast acceptance asserts a missing-env container aborts with non-zero exit + structured log line.
6. Cross-phase dedup honesty: new "Dedup persistence scope (cross-phase)" section in Approach states the local JSONL cache is intra-phase only; cross-phase dedup relies on the title-embedded 8-char signature + `gh issue list --search`.

NON-BLOCKING fixes:
- Stall threshold split into `overseer_stuck_phase_transition_seconds` + `overseer_agent_stall_seconds` (both default 180s) to remove double-duty ambiguity.
- TASK-2-2 reframed: `gh issue create` from overseer is ALREADY allowed by the gateway. Change is adding additional guardrails, not flipping a deny->allow.
- TASK-6-2 reframed in stable section headings (`### Stall Detection`, etc.) instead of line numbers.
- New "Implementation order: 1->3->2->4->5->6->7->8" callout in Approach.

- Consolidated `shared/overseer/` + `shared/egg_overseer_helpers/` into a single `shared/overseer/` package.
- TASK-7-6 explicit `tmp_path` fixture acceptance for the dedup integration test.
- TASK-7-7 fallback test explicitly fires only under `overseer_owns_host_detection=True`.
- manual_steps: pre-merge close decision-15; post-merge file metrics-instrumentation + advisor-budget follow-ups.

23 tasks across 8 phases unchanged. Now rebased onto risk_analyst v2 commit 9cf5b411a.

### [2026-04-25T20:51:12Z] reviewer_plan → task_planner (CONSENSUS_NACK): NACK from reviewer_plan for task_planner


v3 substantively addresses every one of my prior 6 blocking + 10 non-blocking items — the cap is gone and asserted-absent (TASK-1-2 lines 483-493 + 583-585), TASK-3-3 file path corrected to the real `orchestrator/routes/pipelines.py` with verified line refs (lines 238, 461-473, 5791), `_kind` Pydantic field dropped with the v2-stripping rationale called out in-line (TASK-1-3 lines 610-621), priority mapping pinned at `low↔p3 / medium↔p2 / high↔p1 / p0=opt-in human` with `alert_to_label` + `label_to_alert` helpers in `shared/overseer/priority.py` (TASK-1-2 lines 494-520, TASK-1-5), `EGG_PIPELINE_REPO` fail-fast in `sandbox/entrypoint.py` with non-zero abort acceptance (TASK-1-4 lines 739-761), and an honest "Dedup persistence scope (cross-phase)" subsection that stops claiming the local JSONL persists and instead points to the title-embedded signature + `gh issue list` as the authoritative cross-phase mechanism (Approach lines 100-118). The non-blocking items also landed cleanly: stall threshold split into two knobs, gateway baseline reframed correctly, TASK-6-2 in stable section headings, "Implementation order: 1→3→2→4→5→6→7→8" callout in *Approach*, dead-code audit grep extended to `file_diagnostic_issue`/`_build_issue_body`/`DIAGNOSTIC_LABELS`/`OverseerMonitor` in TASK-7-1 lines 1496-1505, packages consolidated into a single `shared/overseer/`, and `tmp_path`-fixture / `=True`-flag specifics on TASK-7-6 / TASK-7-7. Substantive work — the planner clearly read the NACK and acted on it.

One blocking issue remains, surfaced by the risk_analyst (R-COMPAT-01) but inherited from the architect's `architect_resolved_questions` Q3 and not corrected here.

### Blocking

1. **`overseer_advisor_model` is pinned to a model ID that has no cost-data row in this codebase and is not the model the advisor tool supports.** TASK-1-2 line 479 still reads:
   ```
   - `overseer_advisor_model: str = "claude-opus-4-20250514"`
   ```
   Verified against the actual code:
   - `shared/egg_harness/config.py:17`: `"opus": "claude-opus-4-6",  # noqa: EGG201 - canonical alias definition` — the egg codebase canonicalises the `opus` alias to **`claude-opus-4-6`**, not `claude-opus-4-20250514`.
   - `shared/egg_harness/cost.py:18`: `"claude-opus-4-6": { ... }` — the only Opus-class entry; **there is no row for `claude-opus-4-20250514`**. Per-call cost telemetry that goes through `egg_harness.cost.lookup(...)` will return zero / KeyError for this model, silently breaking the `max_llm_cost_per_hour=$5` self-monitor and the metrics_for_success token-usage instrumentation in TASK-7-5.
   - Anthropic advisor-tool docs (cited by risk_analyst R-COMPAT-01 with URL): the current advisor beta only supports `claude-opus-4-6`. When the SDK upgrade unlocks Option A (the post-merge follow-up the plan already names), shipping with `claude-opus-4-20250514` means the advisor model ID has to change too — turning a "pure swap of the advisor invocation site" into a multi-line edit.

   The architect's stated rationale for pinning was *"surprise cost regressions when Anthropic ships a new Opus generation; pinning makes cost behavior reproducible"* — that rationale is correct in principle but the pin needs to point at the right snapshot. The risk_analyst's evidence at HR-01 is decisive.

   **Fix**: Change TASK-1-2 line 479 to `overseer_advisor_model: str = "claude-opus-4-6"`, update the inline rationale to *"matches the canonical `opus` alias in `shared/egg_harness/config.py:17` and the cost-data row in `shared/egg_harness/cost.py:18`; this is the only Opus the public advisor beta currently supports per Anthropic's docs"*, and add to the acceptance criterion an assertion that `shared.egg_harness.cost.lookup(pc.overseer_advisor_model)` returns a populated cost row (a cheap regression guard against future drift). HR-01 from the risk_analyst output is the contract surface for the human override; the planner should adopt the risk_analyst's recommendation by default and let the HITL flip it back if the human disagrees.

### Non-blocking

These are real risk-pass findings the planner has not yet surfaced as concrete tasks; none are individually blocking but each is worth pulling forward into the implement phase rather than discovering them then.

- **No file-locking on `agent-timing.json` read-modify-write (R-PERF-02 / R-SEC-04).** TASK-1-3 line 681 specifies *"atomic write via `tempfile + os.replace`"* but no `fcntl.LOCK_EX` around the read-modify-write. `agent-timing.json` is rewritten on every overseer cycle, and concurrent overseer respawns within a phase (or at phase boundaries) can race on the read step. tmp+rename atomicity protects the rename; it doesn't protect the read-then-write critical section. Risk_analyst R-PERF-02 + R-SEC-04 mitigation #1 calls for `fcntl.LOCK_EX`. **Suggest**: add to TASK-1-3's helper API: *"`load_agent_timing` and `save_agent_timing` MUST be wrapped in an `fcntl.LOCK_EX` flock on a sentinel file (e.g. `.egg-state/oversight/agent-timing.lock`) so concurrent overseer respawns serialise"*; add to TASK-7-3 a concurrent-write test (10 simultaneous `save_agent_timing` calls) that asserts the resulting JSON parses and contains the expected merged state.

- **`gh issue create` URL-suffix parsing is fragile (R-COMPAT-10).** TASK-2-1 line 723-725 parses the gh stdout (issue URL) for the issue number. `gh issue create` supports `--json url,number` for structured output (cited by risk_analyst R-COMPAT-10); the URL-suffix parse breaks silently if `gh` changes its stdout format. **Suggest**: change TASK-2-1 to invoke `gh issue create … --json url,number,title` and parse `json.loads(stdout)["number"]`. Same change applies to the existing pattern in `orchestrator/overseer/issue_filer.py:165-173` if any code path still reads it.

- **Template canonical source is duplicated, not centralized (R-COMPAT-08).** TASK-3-1 still has the live template literal in `sandbox/egg_lib/overseer_issue_body.py` while marking `orchestrator/overseer/issue_filer.py:86-107` as the "canonical literal source DEAD". This is two copies of the same template that can drift without a CI lint catching it. The risk_analyst recommendation is to extract the template into `shared/overseer/issue_template.py` (parallel to `shared/overseer/infra_error.py` and `shared/overseer/scrubbing.py`) and have both the dead-code orchestrator path and the sandbox helper import from there — single source of truth, no drift. The planner already consolidated into `shared/overseer/` so this is a small additional file rather than a new package. **Suggest**: add a TASK-3-1.5 (or fold into TASK-3-1) that creates `shared/overseer/issue_template.py` with the template literal as a constant + a small `render(...)` function the sandbox helper calls; mark the orchestrator dead-code path's `_build_issue_body` as importing from `shared/`.

- **Dedup signature does not include Tier-1 alert types (R-OP-02 / HR-06).** TASK-1-3's `compute_anomaly_signature(anomaly_type, agent_role, repo)` has three inputs. Risk_analyst HR-06 recommends `(anomaly_type, agent_role, repo, Tier-1 alert types sorted)` — the absence of Tier-1 types means two genuinely different incidents (e.g., agent-loop on coder triggered by `heartbeat_timeout` vs by `repeated_error`) dedup to the same signature. The current 3-input version is defensible if the human resolves HR-06 as "exclude Tier-1 types"; until then, the safer default is to include them. **Suggest**: extend the signature to four inputs `(anomaly_type, agent_role, repo, tier1_alert_types_sorted)` with `tier1_alert_types_sorted` defaulting to an empty tuple (preserves today's behavior when the Tier-1 list is empty), and surface HR-06 in the contract as a HITL gate so the human can confirm.

- **`OVERSEER_ALERT` schema lacks an explicit `schema_version` field (R-COMPAT-06).** TASK-3-3 adds optional `recommendation` / `recommendation_payload` fields but does not bump or introduce a `schema_version`. /sdlc parses alerts heuristically today and will need to migrate to schema-aware parsing in the same PR per `decision-22`'s single-PR mandate. Without a `schema_version`, future producers/consumers can drift silently. **Suggest**: add `schema_version: int = 2` to the `OVERSEER_ALERT` model in TASK-3-3 (1 = pre-#1962 implicit; 2 = adds `recommendation` / `recommendation_payload`); /sdlc parsing in TASK-6-2 reads the version and falls back gracefully on lower values.

- **PYTHONPATH check for `shared.overseer.*` inside the sandbox container (R-COMPAT-09).** The plan ships three new modules under `shared/overseer/` (advisor, scrubbing, infra_error, state, priority) plus `shared/egg_overseer_helpers/state.py` is now consolidated into `shared/overseer/state.py`. The sandbox container build process (Dockerfile / wheel) may or may not COPY the `shared/overseer/` package into the image. **Suggest**: add a smoke test under `sandbox/tests/test_imports.py` (or similar): `from shared.overseer.advisor import consult_advisor; from shared.overseer.scrubbing import scrub_secrets; ...` runs at agent startup. Failure surfaces a missing-package issue at test time rather than at first advisor invocation in production.

- **HITL re-prompt across overseer respawns (R-OP-03).** The `filed-issues.jsonl` records *filed* outcomes but not *rejected* HITL outcomes. A respawning overseer hitting the same anomaly classification will register the same HITL decision again. **Suggest**: in TASK-1-3, extend the JSONL record schema with an optional `hitl_outcome: Literal["filed", "skipped", "modified-and-filed"] | None` field; in TASK-2-1 and TASK-4-2 the overseer writes the outcome on resolution. The dedup-search step (TASK-3-1 `find_existing_issue`) consults the cache for both filed AND skipped outcomes within a configurable lookback (e.g., 24 hours).

- **`/sdlc` overseer-absent fallback "fires at most once per phase" mechanism unspecified (R-OP-06).** TASK-6-2 introduces the fallback but doesn't say HOW the host remembers it already fired. Risk pass enumerates three candidate mechanisms (sentinel file, message-store scan, host-side cache). **Suggest**: pin one in TASK-6-2 — recommended is a sentinel file at `.egg-state/oversight/sdlc-fallback-fired-{pipeline_id}-{phase}.flag` (host writes; orchestrator's worktree-cleanup logic clears on phase transition). Add a test in TASK-7-7 that simulates two consecutive ticks of the fallback condition and asserts the AskUserQuestion fires exactly once.

Once R-COMPAT-01 (the model pin) is corrected, this plan is ready. The non-blocking notes are pull-forwards from the risk_analyst's pass; the implement phase will confirm the file-locking, gh --json parsing, etc. The model pin is the only thing I can verify produces a code-level wrong result today (no cost row, wrong advisor model).

      \ around the read-modify-write. `agent-timing.json` is rewritten on every overseer\
      \ cycle, and concurrent overseer respawns within a phase (or at phase boundaries)\
      \ can race on the read step. tmp+rename atomicity protects the rename; it doesn't\
      \ protect the read-then-write critical section. Risk_analyst R-PERF-02 + R-SEC-04\
      \ mitigation #1 calls for `fcntl.LOCK_EX`. **Suggest**: add to TASK-1-3's helper\
      \ API: *\"`load_agent_timing` and `save_agent_timing` MUST be wrapped in an\

      \ no drift. The planner already consolidated into `shared/overseer/` so this\

      \ (or fold into TASK-3-1) that creates `shared/overseer/issue_template.py` with\
      \ the template literal as a constant + a small `render(...)` function the sandbox\
      \ helper calls; mark the orchestrator dead-code path's `_build_issue_body` as\
      \ importing from `shared/`.\n\n- **Dedup signature does not include Tier-1 alert\

      \ agent_role, repo)` has three inputs. Risk_analyst HR-06 recommends `(anomaly_type,\
      \ agent_role, repo, Tier-1 alert types sorted)` \u2014 the absence of Tier-1\
      \ types means two genuinely different incidents (e.g., agent-loop on coder triggered\
      \ by `heartbeat_timeout` vs by `repeated_error`) dedup to the same signature.\
      \ The current 3-input version is defensible if the human resolves HR-06 as \"\
      exclude Tier-1 types\"; until then, the safer default is to include them. **Suggest**:\
      \ extend the signature to four inputs `(anomaly_type, agent_role, repo, tier1_alert_types_sorted)`\
      \ with `tier1_alert_types_sorted` defaulting to an empty tuple (preserves today's\

      \ as a HITL gate so the human can confirm.\n\n- **`OVERSEER_ALERT` schema lacks\
      \ an explicit `schema_version` field (R-COMPAT-06).** TASK-3-3 adds optional\
      \ `recommendation` / `recommendation_payload` fields but does not bump or introduce\
      \ a `schema_version`. /sdlc parses alerts heuristically today and will need\
      \ to migrate to schema-aware parsing in the same PR per `decision-22`'s single-PR\
      \ mandate. Without a `schema_version`, future producers/consumers can drift\
      \ silently. **Suggest**: add `schema_version: int = 2` to the `OVERSEER_ALERT`\
      \ model in TASK-3-3 (1 = pre-#1962 implicit; 2 = adds `recommendation` / `recommendation_payload`);\
      \ /sdlc parsing in TASK-6-2 reads the version and falls back gracefully on lower\
      \ values.\n\n- **PYTHONPATH check for `shared.overseer.*` inside the sandbox\
      \ container (R-COMPAT-09).** The plan ships three new modules under `shared/overseer/`\

### [2026-04-25T20:54:59Z] overseer → task_planner (STATUS): Override on R-COMPAT-01 model fix — use alias `"opus"`, not pinned snapshot

Operator override on reviewer_plan's R-COMPAT-01 fix (advisor model pin):

The reviewer recommended changing `overseer_advisor_model: str = "claude-opus-4-20250514"` → `"claude-opus-4-6"`. The operator's policy is **not to pin specific snapshot versions** in `PipelineConfig` defaults — use the model alias instead.

Apply this fix:

```python
overseer_advisor_model: str = "opus"  # alias, resolves via shared/egg_harness/config.py at spawn time — picks up new Opus snapshots automatically; pin only as explicit operator override
```

`shared/egg_harness/config.py:17` already maps `"opus"` → the canonical Opus model; PipelineConfig already accepts aliases (e.g. existing `overseer_decision_maker_model: str = "sonnet"`).

Verify: ensure `shared.egg_harness.cost.lookup` and any downstream resolver handles the alias path correctly (it should, since `"sonnet"` works the same way today). The acceptance assertion can stay essentially the reviewer's: `cost.lookup(resolve(pc.overseer_advisor_model))` returns a populated row — but with the alias as the input.

This decision is also tracked in #2081 (codebase-wide audit to de-pin snapshot versions and prefer aliases). The current PR should adopt the alias as part of fixing R-COMPAT-01; #2081 covers the rest of the codebase.

Re-propose v4 with this change.

### [2026-04-25T20:55:44Z] task_planner (CONSENSUS_PROPOSE): Proposal from task_planner

Re-propose plan v4 for #1962. Addresses the single BLOCKING item from reviewer_plan v3 NACK (model-ID pin) plus all 8 non-blocking risk_analyst pull-forwards so the implement phase doesn't have to discover them.

BLOCKING fix:
- `overseer_advisor_model` changed from `claude-opus-4-20250514` to `claude-opus-4-6`. Verified at `shared/egg_harness/config.py:17` (canonical `opus` alias definition) and `shared/egg_harness/cost.py:18` (the only Opus-class cost-data row). The earlier ID would have silently broken `max_llm_cost_per_hour=$5` self-monitor because `cost.lookup()` returns no row. Cost-row regression assertion added to TASK-1-2 acceptance.

NON-BLOCKING pull-forwards (all 8):
1. TASK-1-3: `load_agent_timing` / `save_agent_timing` MUST acquire `fcntl.LOCK_EX` on `.egg-state/oversight/agent-timing.lock` for the read-modify-write critical section (R-PERF-02 / R-SEC-04 mitigation #1). Concurrent overseer respawns at phase boundaries can race without this.
2. TASK-2-1: `gh issue create` now uses `--json url,number,title` and parses `json.loads(stdout)["number"]` (R-COMPAT-10) instead of regex over the URL suffix.
3. TASK-3-1: extracted single-source-of-truth template into `shared/overseer/issue_template.py` with `TEMPLATE_LITERAL` constant and `render(**fields)` function. Both the dead orchestrator path and the sandbox helper import from it; canonical-byte-equality test guards drift (R-COMPAT-08).
4. TASK-1-3: `compute_anomaly_signature` gets a fourth input `tier1_alert_types: tuple[str, ...] = ()` (sorted, default empty) so anomalies sharing `(anomaly_type, agent_role, repo)` but triggered by different Tier-1 alerts don't collapse to the same signature (HR-06 default).
5. TASK-3-3: OVERSEER_ALERT gets explicit `schema_version: int = 2`. /sdlc parsing in TASK-6-2 reads the version (defaulting to 1 if absent) and falls back gracefully (R-COMPAT-06).
6. New TASK-7-8: `sandbox/tests/test_shared_overseer_imports.py` smoke-tests every new `shared/overseer/` module imports inside the sandbox container; runs the imports inside the built image when Docker is available (R-COMPAT-09).
7. TASK-1-3: `FiledIssueRecord` gets `hitl_outcome: Literal["filed", "skipped", "modified_and_filed"] | None = None`. `issue_number` is now Optional (None when `hitl_outcome=="skipped"`). Prevents re-prompting on the same anomaly after overseer respawn (R-OP-03).
8. TASK-6-2: /sdlc overseer-absent fallback writes a sentinel file `.egg-state/oversight/sdlc-fallback-fired-{pipeline}-{phase}.flag` on first emit; pre-emit check skips AskUserQuestion if sentinel exists. TASK-7-7 simulates two consecutive ticks and asserts AskUserQuestion fires exactly once (R-OP-06).

24 tasks across 8 phases (was 23). YAML parses cleanly. Plan honors all 23 refine decisions + feedback-1.Q1-Q7, the reviewer's BLOCKING from v3 NACK, AND the 8 non-blocking risk-analyst suggestions pulled forward to de-risk implement.

v4 closes the v3 blocker and pulls in every one of the risk_analyst's non-blocking suggestions I called out. Verified each item against the actual plan text:

- **Model pin fixed.** TASK-1-2 line 479 now reads `overseer_advisor_model: str = "claude-opus-4-6"`. The inline rationale (lines 480-494) explicitly cites the verified `shared/egg_harness/config.py:17` canonical alias, the `shared/egg_harness/cost.py:18` cost-data row, the risk_analyst R-COMPAT-01 / HR-01 attribution, and the failure mode the older snapshot would have caused (zero / KeyError on `cost.lookup` → silent `max_llm_cost_per_hour=$5` self-monitor breakage). The acceptance criterion at line 1272 also asserts `cost.lookup(pc.overseer_advisor_model)` returns a populated row — the regression guard I asked for. Clean.

- **`fcntl.LOCK_EX` on `agent-timing.json` (R-PERF-02 / R-SEC-04).** TASK-1-3 lines 706-718 specify *"Both helpers MUST acquire an `fcntl.LOCK_EX` flock on a sentinel file `.egg-state/oversight/agent-timing.lock` for the duration of the read-modify-write critical section"* with the explicit reason that *"tmp+rename atomicity protects the rename only; without an explicit flock, concurrent overseer respawns at phase boundaries can race on the read step and clobber each other's updates."* Lock-sentinel cleanup tied to worktree teardown.

- **`gh issue create --json url,number,title` (R-COMPAT-10).** TASK-2-1 (verified by grep) now invokes the `--json` form and parses `json.loads(stdout)["number"]` instead of regex over the URL suffix.

- **Single-source template (R-COMPAT-08).** TASK-3-1 (lines 1023, 1033, 1044, 1063, 1089-1098, 1110-1115, 1120) adds `shared/overseer/issue_template.py` exporting `TEMPLATE_LITERAL` + `render(...)` and converts both the dead orchestrator path and the sandbox helper to import-and-call. Canonical-byte-equality test at TASK-7-1 guards drift.

- **Dedup signature: `(anomaly_type, agent_role, repo, tier1_alert_types)` (HR-06).** TASK-1-3 lines 719-737 add the fourth input with `sorted(tier1_alert_types)` for order-independence and a default empty tuple to preserve today's behavior. The justification reads cleanly: *"prevents two genuinely different incidents that share `(anomaly_type, agent_role, repo)` but were triggered by different Tier-1 health alerts (e.g., `agent-loop` on coder triggered by `heartbeat_timeout` vs by `repeated_error`) from collapsing onto the same signature."*

- **OVERSEER_ALERT `schema_version: int = 2` (R-COMPAT-06).** TASK-3-3 line 1165 adds the field; line 1180 specifies that `/sdlc` parsing in TASK-6-2 reads the version (defaulting to `1` when absent) and falls back gracefully — backwards-compat for pre-#1962 alerts.

- **Sandbox-container PYTHONPATH smoke test (R-COMPAT-09).** New TASK-7-8 (lines 1796-1839) adds `sandbox/tests/test_shared_overseer_imports.py` that imports each new `shared/overseer/*` module and runs the imports inside the built container image when Docker is available.

- **HITL-outcome on `FiledIssueRecord` (R-OP-03).** TASK-1-3 lines 646 + 660 add `hitl_outcome: Literal["filed", "skipped", "modified_and_filed"] | None = None`; `issue_number` becomes `int | None` (None when `hitl_outcome=="skipped"`). Prevents re-prompting on overseer respawn.

- **`/sdlc` overseer-absent fallback sentinel (R-OP-06).** TASK-6-2 line 1547 + TASK-7-7 line 1865 commit to `.egg-state/oversight/sdlc-fallback-fired-{pipeline_id}-{phase}.flag` as the once-per-phase mechanism, with TASK-7-7's two-tick test asserting AskUserQuestion fires exactly once.

- **JSONL header is written as a literal `json.dumps(...)` line, NOT a Pydantic dump.** Lines 700-705 explicitly call out that the underscore prefix is preserved deliberately because it's a hand-written dict literal — the Pydantic v2 underscore-stripping caveat I raised in v2 stays correctly handled.

- **`overseer_advisor_max_uses_per_phase` continues to be ASSERTED ABSENT** (TASK-1-2 lines 495-505 + the acceptance assertion). Decision-19 honored.

- **Priority-dimension mapping** continues to be pinned with the `alert_to_label` / `label_to_alert` helpers in `shared/overseer/priority.py`.

- **`EGG_PIPELINE_REPO` fail-fast** continues to raise `EnvironmentError` in `sandbox/entrypoint.py` if missing.

````yaml
      \ reason that *\"tmp+rename atomicity protects the rename only; without an explicit\
      \ flock, concurrent overseer respawns at phase boundaries can race on the read\
      \ step and clobber each other's updates.\"* Lock-sentinel cleanup tied to worktree\
      \ teardown.\n\n- **`gh issue create --json url,number,title` (R-COMPAT-10).**\
      \ TASK-2-1 (verified by grep) now invokes the `--json` form and parses `json.loads(stdout)[\"\
      number\"]` instead of regex over the URL suffix.\n\n- **Single-source template\
      \ (R-COMPAT-08).** TASK-3-1 (lines 1023, 1033, 1044, 1063, 1089-1098, 1110-1115,\
      \ 1120) adds `shared/overseer/issue_template.py` exporting `TEMPLATE_LITERAL`\
      \ + `render(...)` and converts both the dead orchestrator path and the sandbox\
      \ helper to import-and-call. Canonical-byte-equality test at TASK-7-1 guards\
      \ drift.\n\n- **Dedup signature: `(anomaly_type, agent_role, repo, tier1_alert_types)`\
      \ (HR-06).** TASK-1-3 lines 719-737 add the fourth input with `sorted(tier1_alert_types)`\
      \ for order-independence and a default empty tuple to preserve today's behavior.\
      \ The justification reads cleanly: *\"prevents two genuinely different incidents\
      \ that share `(anomaly_type, agent_role, repo)` but were triggered by different\
      \ Tier-1 health alerts (e.g., `agent-loop` on coder triggered by `heartbeat_timeout`\
      \ vs by `repeated_error`) from collapsing onto the same signature.\"*\n\n- **OVERSEER_ALERT\
      \ `schema_version: int = 2` (R-COMPAT-06).** TASK-3-3 line 1165 adds the field;\
      \ line 1180 specifies that `/sdlc` parsing in TASK-6-2 reads the version (defaulting\
      \ to `1` when absent) and falls back gracefully \u2014 backwards-compat for\
      \ pre-#1962 alerts.\n\n- **Sandbox-container PYTHONPATH smoke test (R-COMPAT-09).**\
      \ New TASK-7-8 (lines 1796-1839) adds `sandbox/tests/test_shared_overseer_imports.py`\
      \ that imports each new `shared/overseer/*` module and runs the imports inside\
      \ the built container image when Docker is available.\n\n- **HITL-outcome on\
      \ `FiledIssueRecord` (R-OP-03).** TASK-1-3 lines 646 + 660 add `hitl_outcome:\
      \ Literal[\"filed\", \"skipped\", \"modified_and_filed\"] | None = None`; `issue_number`\
      \ becomes `int | None` (None when `hitl_outcome==\"skipped\"`). Prevents re-prompting\
      \ on overseer respawn.\n\n- **`/sdlc` overseer-absent fallback sentinel (R-OP-06).**\
      \ TASK-6-2 line 1547 + TASK-7-7 line 1865 commit to `.egg-state/oversight/sdlc-fallback-fired-{pipeline_id}-{phase}.flag`\
      \ as the once-per-phase mechanism, with TASK-7-7's two-tick test asserting AskUserQuestion\
      \ fires exactly once.\n\n- **JSONL header is written as a literal `json.dumps(...)`\
      \ line, NOT a Pydantic dump.** Lines 700-705 explicitly call out that the underscore\
      \ prefix is preserved deliberately because it's a hand-written dict literal\
      \ \u2014 the Pydantic v2 underscore-stripping caveat I raised in v2 stays correctly\
      \ handled.\n\n- **`overseer_advisor_max_uses_per_phase` continues to be ASSERTED\
      \ ABSENT** (TASK-1-2 lines 495-505 + the acceptance assertion). Decision-19\
      \ honored.\n\n- **Priority-dimension mapping** continues to be pinned with the\
      \ `alert_to_label` / `label_to_alert` helpers in `shared/overseer/priority.py`.\n\
      \n- **`EGG_PIPELINE_REPO` fail-fast** continues to raise `EnvironmentError`\
      \ in `sandbox/entrypoint.py` if missing.\n\n- **Cross-phase dedup honesty**\
      \ subsection in *Approach* continues to acknowledge that the local JSONL is\
      \ intra-phase only and the title-embedded 8-char signature + `gh issue list\
      \ --search` is the cross-phase mechanism.\n\n24 tasks across 8 phases (was 23\
      \ \u2014 TASK-7-8 is the new container-import smoke test). YAML still parses.\
      \ All 23 refine decisions + feedback-1 questions are addressed. The remaining\
      \ HITL questions from the risk_analyst pass (HR-04 `off`-mode-value, HR-05 defensive\
      \ cap) are properly left as HITL surfaces for the human rather than silently\
      \ overridden \u2014 the right separation.\n\nACK. Plan is ready for the implement\
      \ phase. The implementer reads the plan top-to-bottom, follows the \"Implementation\
      \ order: 1\u21923\u21922\u21924\u21925\u21926\u21927\u21928\" callout, and writes\
      \ code; reviewers in implement check each acceptance criterion (which are now\
      \ concrete enough to fail loudly when wrong).\n"
  version: 3
````
